MICHIGAN LIFE PRODUCER
16-65
2026 FINAL MASTER BLUEPRINT

One self-contained page for the Michigan Life Producer 16-65 exam. The study material, Michigan rules, concept explanations, trap pairs, practice bank, and full mock exams are all printed in this file. No outside page is required to study the content.

Accuracy promise, not a fake score promise: No study guide can honestly guarantee a specific PSI score because PSI uses multiple forms and a confidential item bank. This system is designed to cover the full public 16-65 blueprint and train transfer to unfamiliar wording. The internal readiness standard is 90%+ on two fresh, closed-book 100-question forms, with no domain below 85%.
How to use: Learn the guide first. Then answer the 440 bank without looking at the green answer box. Finish with the three 100-question forms closed-book. Any answer you guessed counts as a miss during remediation.

Why this rebuild is weighted the way it is

Agency failure-pattern analytics

The detailed PSI score reports supplied for three attempts were 53, 47, and 58. Across those reports, the three largest knowledge domains — Life Basics (21), Life Policies (12), and Provisions/Options/Riders (21) — account for 54 of the 100 scored questions and repeatedly produced major point loss. That is why this guide gives those sections disproportionate depth rather than over-focusing on Michigan deadline memorization.

These are internal diagnostic results, not statewide performance statistics. They are used only to decide where this study system needs extra depth.

Current-law supplement — verified September 25, 2026

5. Michigan rules that deserve their own memory pass

These are current Michigan licensing/sales rules that overlap the public Life Producer blueprint or are close enough to the tested regulation/annuity material that they belong in a final-prep system.

Resident prelicensing / exam: Michigan requires approved prelicensing education before the Life Producer 16-65 exam. DIFS currently states the Life line may be met by an approved 20-hour classroom course or approved self-study course. The PE completion certificate is valid 12 months. A passing exam result is also valid 12 months. A resident license application in DIFS' database is valid 180 days; if the exam is not passed within 180 days, a new application/fee is required.
Appointments: A producer may not act as an insurer's agent unless appointed. The insurer files an appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted. DIFS verifies eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days; if ineligible, the insurer is notified within 5 days after that determination.
Termination for cause: Insurer reports the termination within 30 days after its effective date; the insurer sends the producer a copy within 15 days after notifying DIFS; the producer generally has 30 days to submit written comments.
Continuing education: Resident producers generally complete 24 approved credits every 2 years, including at least 3 ethics credits. After meeting the minimum, up to 12 extra credits may carry forward under current DIFS rules. The life-license exam itself tests the core 24/2 and 3-ethics numbers most often.
Annuity training / best interest: A Michigan producer licensed on or after June 29, 2021 must complete a one-time 4-hour approved annuity training course before selling annuities. When recommending an annuity, the producer must act in the consumer's best interest under the circumstances known at the time and collect relevant consumer-profile information. Insurers also provide product-specific training.
Variable annuity / variable life: Variable products are securities as well as insurance products. Michigan's current licensing page requires the appropriate variable authority plus FINRA registration and securities examinations; the Life Producer exam still tests the SEC/FINRA/Michigan regulatory distinction conceptually.
Long-term-care training: Producers selling/soliciting/negotiating LTC coverage need the proper life or accident-and-health producer authority, a one-time 8-hour LTC-Partnership training course, and at least 4 hours of ongoing LTC training in each 2-year CE compliance period.
Advertising: Michigan DIFS emphasizes that life/annuity advertising must identify the insurer, be truthful, clearly identify the product, and not mislead consumers. A slogan cannot be more prominent than the insurer name under the applicable rules.
Brokerage: DIFS' appointment guidance states that “brokerage” is not allowed in Michigan in the sense of a broker representing the consumer rather than an insurer. For exam purposes, focus on the appointed producer/insurer agency relationship and fiduciary handling of premiums.

Current-law items can change. This page was source-checked against Michigan DIFS, the Michigan Insurance Code, and IRS material on September 25, 2026.

6. Confusion pairs / PSI traps

EXPRESS vs IMPLIED vs APPARENT

Express = insurer specifically grants it. Implied = not stated but reasonably necessary to do the granted job. Apparent = insurer's conduct makes a third party reasonably believe authority exists.

FIDUCIARY vs AUTHORITY

Fiduciary describes responsibility for entrusted money/property. It is not one of the three agency-authority categories.

EXTENDED TERM vs REDUCED PAID-UP

Extended term = original face amount, temporary term. Reduced paid-up = smaller face amount, permanent, no more premiums.

FIXED PERIOD vs FIXED AMOUNT

Fixed period fixes HOW LONG. Fixed amount fixes HOW MUCH each payment is.

OWNER vs INSURED vs BENEFICIARY vs ANNUITANT

Owner controls contract. Insured is the life covered. Beneficiary receives proceeds. Annuitant is the measuring life for annuity payouts.

FIXED vs VARIABLE ANNUITY

Fixed = insurer bears investment risk/general account. Variable = owner bears investment risk/separate account.

CREDIT LIFE vs KEY PERSON vs BUY-SELL

Credit life = borrower debt. Key person = business protects against loss of key worker. Buy-sell = funds transfer of deceased owner's business interest.

APS vs MIB vs INSPECTION REPORT vs PRODUCER REPORT

APS = doctor's detail. MIB = coded member-insurer alert. Inspection report = third-party background/lifestyle. Producer report = producer observations.

TWISTING vs REBATING vs MISREPRESENTATION

Twisting = misleading replacement. Rebating = unlisted incentive. Misrepresentation = false/misleading policy statement more generally.

DEATH BENEFIT vs INTEREST

Death benefit is generally income-tax-free. Interest earned on retained/delayed proceeds is taxable.

ANNUITY WITHDRAWAL vs ANNUITIZATION

Pre-annuitization nonqualified withdrawal with gain is generally earnings-first (LIFO). Annuitized payments use a return-of-basis/taxable-earnings allocation.

PARTICIPATING vs NONPARTICIPATING

Participating MAY pay dividends if declared; not guaranteed. Nonparticipating does not pay policy dividends.

REVOCABLE vs IRREVOCABLE BENEFICIARY

Revocable can generally be changed without consent. Irrevocable generally has rights requiring consent to affected changes.

JOINT LIFE vs SURVIVORSHIP

Joint life = first death pays. Survivorship = second death pays.

GRACE vs REINSTATEMENT

Grace means policy is still in force while a late premium can be paid. Reinstatement restores qualifying lapsed coverage after grace has ended.

REPRESENTATION vs CONCEALMENT

Representation = applicant statement believed true. Concealment = material fact intentionally withheld.

Wording decoder

7. PSI trigger language — what the stem is really asking

“Specifically stated / written authority”EXPRESS authority
“Necessary to carry out authorized duties; not written”IMPLIED authority
“Customer reasonably believes because of insurer's conduct”APPARENT authority
“Handling premium money in trust”FIDUCIARY responsibility
“Misleading comparison causes replacement”TWISTING
“Something of value not in the contract to induce sale”REBATING / illegal inducement
“Loss or no loss; no chance of gain”PURE risk
“Loss, no loss, or gain”SPECULATIVE risk
“Same face amount; temporary; no more premiums”EXTENDED TERM
“Smaller face amount; permanent; no more premiums”REDUCED PAID-UP
“How much each payment?”FIXED AMOUNT settlement
“How long are payments?”FIXED PERIOD settlement
“Payments for as long as person lives”LIFE INCOME
“Borrower / lender / remaining debt”CREDIT LIFE
“Business loses money if employee dies”KEY PERSON
“Partners/owners need money to buy deceased owner's interest”BUY-SELL funding
“Two insureds; pays first death”JOINT LIFE / first-to-die
“Two insureds; pays second death”SURVIVORSHIP / second-to-die
“Flexible premium + adjustable death benefit”UNIVERSAL LIFE
“Separate account + owner bears investment risk”VARIABLE product
“General account + insurer bears investment risk”FIXED annuity
“Money growing / going in”ACCUMULATION period
“Value converted to periodic income”ANNUITIZATION / annuity period
“Withdrawal from nonqualified annuity before annuitization”EARNINGS FIRST (LIFO), then basis
“Death proceeds in lump sum”Generally income-tax-free
“Interest earned on death proceeds”Generally taxable income
“Statement believed true to best knowledge”REPRESENTATION
“Intentional omission of material fact”CONCEALMENT
“Owner can change beneficiary without consent”REVOCABLE
“Beneficiary has vested rights / consent needed”IRREVOCABLE
“Additional coverage later without evidence”GUARANTEED INSURABILITY
“Disability excuses future premiums”WAIVER OF PREMIUM
“Detailed information from treating physician”APS
“Industry database flags undisclosed medical/application history”MIB
“Consumer character/reputation/lifestyle investigation”INSPECTION / investigative consumer report

8. Full master guide — every domain expanded

No tabs. No hidden cards. Every official-outline checklist and every concept card is printed below.

17 scored questions

Insurance Regulation

17%

Michigan-specific licensing, appointments, producer conduct, reporting, discipline, unfair trade practices, fiduciary duties, fraud, and privacy. This is the section where exact timelines can matter.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Company regulation: producer appointment and termination of appointment
  • Producer duties and commissions
  • Types of licensees: producer, counselor, business entity
  • Maintenance/duration: name/address change, reporting actions, assumed names, continuing education
  • Disciplinary actions: cease/desist, suspension, revocation, refusal to issue/renew, penalties/fines
  • Unfair trade practices: misrepresentation, false advertising, twisting, false statements, boycott/coercion/intimidation, unfair discrimination, rebating, illegal inducement
  • Fiduciary responsibilities, forgery, insurance fraud, consumer privacy

Complete concept guide

1

Producer appointment — 15 days

An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.

PSI clue: Agency contract or first application starts a 15-day filing clock.

2

Appointment eligibility — 30 days

After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.

PSI clue: DIFS eligibility check: think 30.

3

Ineligible appointment notice — 5 days

If the producer is found ineligible for appointment, the director notifies the insurer within 5 days of that determination.

PSI clue: Failed eligibility determination = 5-day notice.

4

Termination reporting — 30 days

An insurer generally reports termination of a producer business relationship within 30 days after the effective date.

PSI clue: Insurer ends relationship → 30-day report.

5

Termination copy to producer — 15 days

After filing certain termination notices, the insurer sends a copy to the producer within 15 days.

PSI clue: Termination filed → producer gets copy: 15.

6

Producer comments on termination — 30 days

A producer may submit written comments concerning a termination notice within 30 days after receiving it.

PSI clue: Producer response/comment window: 30.

7

Name/address change — 30 days

A licensee reports a legal name or address change within 30 days.

PSI clue: Move or legal-name change → 30.

8

Continuing education — 24 hours / 2 years

A resident producer generally completes 24 hours of approved continuing education in each 2-year review period.

PSI clue: CE = 24 in 2.

9

Ethics CE — 3 hours

At least 3 of the 24 continuing-education hours must be ethics in insurance.

PSI clue: 24 total; 3 ethics.

10

Administrative action report — 30 days

A producer reports a covered administrative action within 30 days after final disposition.

PSI clue: Administrative final disposition → 30.

11

Criminal prosecution report — 30 days

A producer reports a criminal prosecution within 30 days after the initial pretrial hearing date.

PSI clue: Criminal prosecution → 30 after initial pretrial hearing.

12

Fiduciary responsibility

Premiums and other money received in a producer's professional role must be handled in trust and not converted to personal use.

PSI clue: Fiduciary = funds held for someone else.

13

Misrepresentation

Making a false or misleading statement about policy terms, benefits, or advantages to induce a transaction is prohibited.

PSI clue: False policy claim used to sell = misrepresentation.

14

Twisting

Using misleading comparisons or misrepresentations to induce a policyowner to lapse, surrender, forfeit, or replace existing coverage is twisting.

PSI clue: Misleading replacement = twisting.

15

Rebating

Offering an inducement or valuable benefit not specified in the contract as an incentive to buy insurance is generally rebating/illegal inducement.

PSI clue: Off-the-contract incentive = rebate.

16

False advertising

Insurance advertising may not contain statements that are false, deceptive, or misleading about an insurer or policy.

PSI clue: Ad must tell the truth.

17

Defamation / false statements

Knowingly making false statements that damage another insurer's financial condition or reputation is an unfair practice.

PSI clue: False attack on competitor = defamation-type unfair practice.

18

Boycott, coercion, intimidation

A person may not use boycott, coercion, or intimidation to create or maintain an unreasonable restraint of insurance trade.

PSI clue: Pressure that restrains insurance trade = prohibited.

19

Unfair discrimination

Insurers may not unfairly discriminate between individuals of the same class and equal life expectancy in rates, benefits, or contract terms.

PSI clue: Same class + equal life expectancy should receive fair treatment.

20

Forgery

Forging another person's name on an insurance-related document is grounds for producer discipline.

PSI clue: Fake signature = serious licensing violation.

21

Insurance fraud

Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.

PSI clue: Material lie to obtain insurance/benefit = fraud issue.

22

Consumer privacy

Nonpublic personal and insurance information is subject to privacy, notice, and disclosure restrictions.

PSI clue: Protect nonpublic consumer information.

23

Cease-and-desist order

The director may order a person to stop conduct that violates insurance law or an applicable order/rule.

PSI clue: Cease and desist = stop the prohibited conduct.

24

License discipline

Grounds can include material misstatements, fraud, misappropriation, dishonest practices, or violating insurance laws and orders.

PSI clue: Bad application info, theft/fraud, or insurance-law violations can jeopardize the license.

25

Commissions

Commissions for insurance sales are generally paid only as permitted by licensing law; unlicensed persons may not be paid for activities requiring a producer license.

PSI clue: License-required activity → compensation rules matter.

18 scored questions

General Insurance

18%

Core insurance logic: risk, insurer structure, agency authority, contract formation, and legal doctrines. PSI often tests close contrasts rather than definitions alone.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Risk, loss, and risk-management methods: avoidance, retention, sharing, transfer
  • Elements of insurable risks; adverse selection; law of large numbers
  • Types of insurers: stock and mutual
  • Producer-insurer relationship; express, implied, apparent authority; duties to applicant/insured
  • Contract elements: offer/acceptance, consideration, competent parties, legal purpose
  • Insurance-contract characteristics and legal interpretations: aleatory, adhesion, unilateral, conditional, personal, indemnity concepts, warranties/representations, concealment, fraud, waiver, estoppel

Complete concept guide

1

Risk

Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.

PSI clue: Uncertainty of loss = risk.

2

Pure risk

Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.

PSI clue: Loss or no loss; no upside.

3

Speculative risk

Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.

PSI clue: Gain is possible = speculative.

4

Avoidance

Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.

PSI clue: Don't do the risky activity.

5

Retention

Risk retention means keeping responsibility for some or all of a potential loss.

PSI clue: You keep the risk.

6

Sharing

Risk sharing distributes potential loss among multiple parties.

PSI clue: Multiple parties share the burden.

7

Transfer

Risk transfer shifts the financial consequences of loss to another party; insurance is a classic method.

PSI clue: Buy insurance → transfer.

8

Insurable risk

An insurable risk is generally definite, measurable, accidental from the insured's standpoint, economically feasible, and part of a large enough pool for prediction.

PSI clue: Definite + measurable + accidental + large pool.

9

Adverse selection

Adverse selection is the tendency of people with greater-than-average risk to seek or keep insurance more readily.

PSI clue: Higher-risk people are more eager to insure.

10

Law of large numbers

As the number of similar exposure units increases, actual losses tend to become more predictable relative to expected losses.

PSI clue: Bigger similar pool = better prediction.

11

Stock insurer

A stock insurer is owned by shareholders, who provide capital and may receive profits as dividends on stock.

PSI clue: Stock = shareholders.

12

Mutual insurer

A mutual insurer is owned by policyholders; participating policyholders may be eligible for non-guaranteed policy dividends.

PSI clue: Mutual = policyholders.

13

Express authority

Express authority is specifically granted to a producer by the insurer, often in the agency agreement.

PSI clue: Explicitly granted = express.

14

Implied authority

Implied authority is not expressly stated but is reasonably necessary to carry out express authority.

PSI clue: Not written but needed for the job = implied.

15

Apparent authority

Apparent authority exists when the insurer's actions cause a third party reasonably to believe the producer has authority.

PSI clue: Insurer makes it appear authorized.

16

Offer and acceptance

A contract requires an offer and an acceptance that agree on the essential terms.

PSI clue: One side proposes; the other accepts.

17

Consideration

Consideration is the value exchanged. In life insurance, the applicant's premium and statements are consideration; the insurer's promise is its consideration.

PSI clue: Something of value from each contracting side.

18

Competent parties

Contracting parties must have legal capacity to contract.

PSI clue: Legal capacity is required.

19

Legal purpose

A contract must have a lawful purpose.

PSI clue: Illegal purpose = no valid contract.

20

Aleatory contract

An insurance contract is aleatory because the values exchanged may be unequal and depend on an uncertain event.

PSI clue: Unequal exchange tied to uncertainty.

21

Adhesion

Insurance contracts are contracts of adhesion because the insurer drafts the policy and the applicant generally accepts or rejects it rather than negotiating wording.

PSI clue: Insurer writes it; insured adheres.

22

Unilateral

Insurance is unilateral because only the insurer makes a legally enforceable promise to perform after the insured meets the contract conditions.

PSI clue: One enforceable promise: insurer.

23

Conditional contract

The insurer's obligation to perform depends on specified policy conditions being satisfied.

PSI clue: Coverage/payment depends on conditions.

24

Personal contract

Life insurance is personal in the sense that underwriting and the contract are based on the individual risk and relationship, not merely property ownership.

PSI clue: Contract centers on the person/risk.

25

Representation

An application statement made to the best of the applicant's knowledge is generally treated as a representation rather than an absolute warranty.

PSI clue: Substantially true to best knowledge = representation.

26

Warranty

A warranty is a statement or promise that is guaranteed to be literally true; insurance applications usually treat statements as representations instead.

PSI clue: Absolute guarantee of truth = warranty.

27

Concealment

Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.

PSI clue: Material fact intentionally hidden.

28

Fraud

Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.

PSI clue: Intentional deceit for gain.

29

Waiver

Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.

PSI clue: Know the right, choose to give it up.

30

Estoppel

Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.

PSI clue: Prior conduct blocks later inconsistent position.

21 scored questions

Life Insurance Basics

21%

The broadest life foundation: Michigan life rules, needs analysis, business uses, underwriting, information sources, risk classes, and producer responsibilities.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Michigan life laws: insurable interest; variable-product regulation; solicitation/sales presentations; advertising; guaranty association; illustrations; policy summary; buyer's guides; replacement; privacy; selection criteria/unfair discrimination
  • Personal uses: survivor protection, estate creation, cash accumulation, security, liquidity, estate conservation
  • Determining amount: human life value, needs approach, information gathered, lump-sum needs, income needs
  • Business uses: buy-sell funding and key person
  • Classes: group vs individual; permanent vs term; participating vs nonparticipating; fixed vs variable
  • Premium payment mode
  • Producer responsibilities: field underwriting, applications/conditional receipts, delivery, policy review, effective date, premium collection, statement of good health
  • Insurer underwriting sources: application, producer report, APS, inspection report, MIB, medical exams/labs including HIV
  • Risk classification: preferred, standard, substandard

Complete concept guide

1

Insurable interest

For life insurance, insurable interest must generally exist when the policy is issued; the policyowner must face a genuine financial or close-family interest in the insured's continued life.

PSI clue: Life insurable interest is tested at policy inception.

2

Variable product regulation

Variable life and variable annuity products involve securities regulation in addition to insurance regulation; securities licensing/registration rules can apply.

PSI clue: Variable = insurance + securities oversight.

3

Solicitation and sales presentations

Sales presentations must be truthful and not misleading, and required disclosures must be provided.

PSI clue: Sell accurately; disclose properly.

4

Life insurance advertising

Life insurance advertising must not create false impressions about benefits, costs, guarantees, or an insurer's identity.

PSI clue: Ads cannot exaggerate guarantees.

5

Guaranty association

The guaranty association is a safety-net mechanism for covered obligations of insolvent member insurers, subject to statutory limits; it is not a sales guarantee or reason to recommend a policy.

PSI clue: Backstop for insolvency, not a marketing promise.

6

Illustration

A life insurance illustration shows policy values under stated assumptions and must distinguish guaranteed from non-guaranteed elements.

PSI clue: Guaranteed vs non-guaranteed must stay clear.

7

Policy summary

A policy summary presents key information about the proposed policy in a standardized consumer-oriented form.

PSI clue: Policy-at-a-glance document.

8

Buyer's guide

A buyer's guide is consumer education intended to help compare and understand life insurance or annuity products.

PSI clue: Consumer education before/around purchase.

9

Replacement

Replacement occurs when new life insurance is purchased and an existing policy is or will be lapsed, surrendered, forfeited, assigned, or otherwise materially affected as defined by replacement rules.

PSI clue: New policy causes old policy to be dropped/changed.

10

Privacy / insurance information

Applicants and insureds have protections concerning collection, use, and disclosure of insurance information.

PSI clue: Information handling is regulated.

11

Survivor protection

Life insurance can replace income or provide funds for dependents after the insured's death.

PSI clue: Protect people who rely on the insured.

12

Estate creation

Life insurance can create an immediate estate by providing a death benefit even if the insured has accumulated few assets.

PSI clue: Creates money at death.

13

Cash accumulation

Permanent life insurance can build cash value that may be accessed subject to policy terms.

PSI clue: Permanent policy can build living value.

14

Liquidity

Life insurance can provide cash at death when an estate may otherwise hold illiquid assets.

PSI clue: Death creates cash when assets are tied up.

15

Estate conservation

Life insurance can supply funds to help preserve estate assets rather than forcing their sale to meet expenses or obligations.

PSI clue: Use insurance cash so assets need not be sold.

16

Human life value approach

The human life value approach estimates the present value of an individual's future economic contribution to dependents.

PSI clue: Income-producing capacity → present value.

17

Needs approach

The needs approach estimates specific survivor lump-sum and income needs, then subtracts available assets and resources.

PSI clue: List needs, subtract resources.

18

Buy-sell funding

Life insurance can fund the purchase of a deceased owner's business interest under a buy-sell agreement.

PSI clue: Business ownership transfer at death.

19

Key person insurance

A business may own and benefit from life insurance on a key employee whose death would cause financial loss.

PSI clue: Business protects itself from loss of a key worker.

20

Group vs individual life

Group life covers eligible members under a master contract; individual life is issued to a specific policyowner on an individual contract.

PSI clue: Master policy vs individual contract.

21

Permanent vs term

Term provides temporary death protection without cash value; permanent coverage is designed to last for life and commonly builds cash value.

PSI clue: Temporary/no cash vs lifetime/cash value.

22

Participating policy

A participating policy may pay policy dividends if declared; dividends are not guaranteed.

PSI clue: Participating = possible dividends.

23

Nonparticipating policy

A nonparticipating policy does not pay policy dividends.

PSI clue: No policy dividends.

24

Fixed vs variable

In fixed products the insurer generally bears investment risk; in variable products the contract owner bears investment risk through separate-account performance.

PSI clue: Fixed = insurer risk; variable = owner risk.

25

Premium mode

Premium mode is the frequency of payment, such as annual, semiannual, quarterly, or monthly; more frequent modes can produce a higher total annual outlay.

PSI clue: Mode = how often premium is paid.

26

Field underwriting

The producer observes and reports relevant applicant information, completes the application accurately, and helps the insurer obtain information; the producer does not make the final underwriting decision.

PSI clue: Producer gathers; insurer decides.

27

Application

The application is a primary source of underwriting information and becomes part of the contract when attached as required.

PSI clue: Applicant answers start underwriting.

28

Conditional receipt

A conditional receipt may make coverage effective as of a specified date if the applicant satisfies the receipt's conditions.

PSI clue: Coverage can predate delivery if conditions are met.

29

Policy delivery

At delivery, the producer should explain the policy, collect any required premium, and obtain any required statement of continued good health if circumstances require it.

PSI clue: Delivery = explain, collect, confirm conditions.

30

Statement of good health

When required, a statement of good health confirms no material health change before coverage becomes effective or a policy is delivered.

PSI clue: Health changed before delivery? This can matter.

31

Producer report

The producer report supplies underwriting observations not necessarily answered by the applicant, such as relevant personal or financial information observed by the producer.

PSI clue: Producer's own underwriting observations.

32

APS

An Attending Physician Statement provides detailed medical information from an applicant's physician when the insurer needs more health detail.

PSI clue: Doctor's detailed report = APS.

33

Inspection report

An investigative consumer or inspection report may contain information about character, reputation, finances, habits, or lifestyle, subject to consumer-reporting rules.

PSI clue: Third-party background/lifestyle report.

34

MIB

The Medical Information Bureau provides coded information reported by member insurers that can alert an underwriter to possible undisclosed medical or application history; it does not make the underwriting decision.

PSI clue: MIB flags inconsistencies; insurer investigates.

35

Medical exam / lab testing

When an insurer requires a medical examination or lab test for underwriting, the insurer generally bears the cost.

PSI clue: Insurer-requested exam → insurer pays.

36

Preferred risk

Preferred applicants present lower-than-average expected mortality and may qualify for more favorable rates.

PSI clue: Better-than-average mortality risk.

37

Standard risk

Standard applicants present average expected mortality for the insurer's normal rating assumptions.

PSI clue: Average mortality risk.

38

Substandard risk

Substandard applicants present higher-than-average expected mortality but may still be insurable with a rating, extra premium, or other terms.

PSI clue: Higher risk, still potentially insurable.

12 scored questions

Life Insurance Policies

12%

Know what each policy actually does. The exam can describe a client's objective and ask you to identify the policy without naming the key term.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Term life: level, renewable, level-premium/convertible concepts, decreasing term where applicable
  • Whole life: straight/continuous premium, limited pay, single premium, graded premium, modified life, interest-sensitive, equity-index concepts
  • Flexible-premium policies: adjustable life and universal life
  • Group life: plan characteristics and group underwriting
  • Policy law: group conversion and universal-life statutory provisions
  • Specialized concepts commonly tested with policy types: joint life, survivorship life, juvenile and credit life distinctions

Complete concept guide

1

Level term

Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.

PSI clue: Term coverage with level face amount.

2

Renewable term

Renewable term permits renewal for another term without new evidence of insurability, subject to contract limits and higher attained-age premiums.

PSI clue: Renew without new medical proof.

3

Convertible term

Convertible term permits conversion to a permanent policy without new evidence of insurability, within contract limits.

PSI clue: Term → permanent without proving insurability again.

4

Decreasing term

Decreasing term has a death benefit that declines over time and is often used for decreasing obligations such as debt.

PSI clue: Face amount goes down.

5

Straight/continuous premium whole life

Straight life whole life uses level premiums payable for life and provides permanent death protection with guaranteed cash value according to the contract.

PSI clue: Pay level premiums for life.

6

Limited-pay whole life

Limited-pay whole life becomes paid-up after premiums are paid for a specified shorter period while coverage continues for life.

PSI clue: Pay premiums for fewer years; coverage continues.

7

Single-premium whole life

Single-premium whole life is funded by one large premium and is then paid-up, subject to tax rules such as MEC treatment if applicable.

PSI clue: One premium funds permanent coverage.

8

Graded premium whole life

Graded premium policies start with lower premiums that increase on a schedule before leveling at a higher amount.

PSI clue: Premium rises in steps.

9

Modified whole life

Modified life typically uses a lower premium for an initial period followed by a higher level premium thereafter.

PSI clue: Low at first, then jumps to a higher level.

10

Interest-sensitive whole life

Interest-sensitive whole life reflects current interest or mortality assumptions within contract limits while retaining whole-life characteristics.

PSI clue: Whole life values can reflect current assumptions.

11

Equity-indexed life

Equity-indexed life credits interest using a formula linked to a market index, subject to contract caps, participation rates, floors, and other terms; the policy is not direct ownership of the index.

PSI clue: Index-linked crediting, not direct stock ownership.

12

Adjustable life

Adjustable life allows changes to items such as premium, face amount, or coverage period within policy limits, blending term/permanent flexibility.

PSI clue: Policy can be adjusted as needs change.

13

Universal life

Universal life is flexible-premium permanent insurance with an account value, mortality/expense charges, and an adjustable death benefit subject to contract rules.

PSI clue: Flexible premium + adjustable death benefit + account value.

14

Group life master contract

In group life, the policyholder (often an employer) owns the master contract and covered individuals receive certificates of insurance.

PSI clue: Employer/organization holds master policy.

15

Group underwriting

Group life underwriting focuses primarily on the group as a whole rather than full individual evidence of insurability for each member under normal enrollment rules.

PSI clue: Underwrite the group, not each person like individual life.

16

Group conversion — 31 days

When qualifying group life coverage terminates, an eligible employee generally has 31 days to apply for an individual policy without new evidence of insurability, subject to statutory terms.

PSI clue: Group ends → 31-day conversion.

17

Credit life

Credit life is designed to pay or reduce a covered borrower's debt if the insured borrower dies.

PSI clue: Borrower + lender + remaining debt.

18

Joint life / first-to-die

Joint life covers two lives and pays the death benefit at the first insured's death.

PSI clue: Two insureds; first death pays.

19

Survivorship / second-to-die

Survivorship life covers two lives and pays after the second insured dies.

PSI clue: Two insureds; second death pays.

20

Juvenile life

Juvenile life insures a child's life and may include a payor benefit rider to protect premium payments if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined.

PSI clue: Child insured; payor rider may protect premiums.

21 scored questions

Life Insurance Policy Provisions, Options, and Riders

21%

The heaviest trap section: ownership, assignments, beneficiaries, settlement options, nonforfeiture, dividends, loans, and riders. Distinguish similar choices instantly.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Standard provisions: ownership, assignment, entire contract, modifications, free look, premium payment, grace period, reinstatement, incontestability, misstatement age/gender, exclusions/restrictions, interest on proceeds
  • Beneficiaries: individuals, classes, estates, minors, trusts, succession, revocable vs irrevocable
  • Settlement options: cash, interest-only, fixed-period, fixed-amount, life income, single life, joint and survivor
  • Nonforfeiture: cash surrender, extended term, reduced paid-up
  • Policy loans/withdrawals: cash loans, automatic premium loans, withdrawals/partial surrenders
  • Dividend options: cash, premium reduction, accumulation at interest, one-year term, paid-up additions
  • Disability riders: waiver of premium/stipulated premium, waiver of cost of insurance, disability income, payor benefit
  • Living benefits: accelerated and long-term-care provisions; conditions and effect on death benefit
  • Additional-insured riders: spouse/other-insured, children's term, family term
  • Death-benefit riders: accidental death, guaranteed insurability, cost of living, return of premium

Complete concept guide

1

Ownership

The policyowner controls contractual rights such as naming/changing a revocable beneficiary, assigning the policy, borrowing against cash value, and selecting options, subject to the contract.

PSI clue: Owner controls the contract.

2

Absolute assignment

Absolute assignment transfers all ownership rights in the policy to another party.

PSI clue: All rights transferred.

3

Collateral assignment

Collateral assignment transfers limited policy rights to a creditor as security for a debt; the assignee's interest is generally limited to the debt.

PSI clue: Policy used as loan collateral.

4

Entire contract

The policy together with the attached application and applicable riders/endorsements constitutes the entire contract.

PSI clue: Policy + attached application = contract.

5

Modifications

Policy changes generally must meet the contract's modification requirements and be made by an authorized officer rather than informally by a producer.

PSI clue: Producer cannot casually rewrite the contract.

6

Free look — at least 10 days

Michigan individual life policies generally provide at least 10 days after receipt to return the policy for cancellation and refund under the statutory right-to-examine rule.

PSI clue: 10-day minimum free look.

7

Grace period — 1 month

After the first policy year, Michigan individual life policies generally include a 1-month grace period for premium payment while coverage continues, subject to statutory terms.

PSI clue: Late premium: 1 month grace.

8

Termination notice — 30 days

Michigan law generally requires written notice at least 30 days before termination of coverage under the life-policy rule, subject to an exception for certain in-person premium collection.

PSI clue: 30-day written notice before termination.

9

Reinstatement — 3 years

A qualifying lapsed life policy can generally be reinstated within 3 years under the statute, with satisfactory evidence of insurability and payment of arrears plus interest.

PSI clue: Reinstate window = 3 years.

10

Incontestability — 2 years

An individual life policy generally becomes incontestable after it has been in force during the insured's lifetime for 2 years, subject to statutory exceptions such as nonpayment of premium.

PSI clue: Contestability clock = 2 years.

11

Misstatement of age or gender

If age or gender is misstated, benefits are generally adjusted to the amount the paid premium would have purchased at the correct age or gender, rather than automatically voiding the policy.

PSI clue: Wrong age → adjust benefit, not automatic cancellation.

12

Exclusions and restrictions

Life policies may contain lawful exclusions or restrictions such as certain aviation, war/military, or rider-specific limitations, but exclusions must be stated in the contract.

PSI clue: Exclusion must come from policy language/law.

13

Interest on proceeds

If an insurer holds proceeds and pays interest, the interest is separate from the death benefit and may have different tax treatment.

PSI clue: Death proceeds and interest are not the same tax item.

14

Primary beneficiary

The primary beneficiary has first claim to policy proceeds when the insured dies.

PSI clue: First in line.

15

Contingent beneficiary

The contingent beneficiary receives proceeds if the primary beneficiary cannot or does not receive them under the policy terms.

PSI clue: Backup beneficiary.

16

Revocable beneficiary

A revocable beneficiary can generally be changed by the policyowner without that beneficiary's consent.

PSI clue: Revocable = owner can change.

17

Irrevocable beneficiary

An irrevocable beneficiary has vested rights and generally must consent to changes that affect those rights.

PSI clue: Irrevocable = consent matters.

18

Class designation

A beneficiary can be designated by a class, such as 'children,' with proceeds distributed according to policy/law at the insured's death.

PSI clue: Beneficiary described as a group.

19

Estate as beneficiary

If the estate is beneficiary, proceeds become part of the estate and may be exposed to estate administration and creditor issues.

PSI clue: Estate beneficiary means proceeds enter the estate.

20

Cash settlement

The insurer pays the proceeds in a lump sum.

PSI clue: All proceeds at once.

21

Interest-only settlement

The insurer retains the principal and pays interest to the beneficiary according to the settlement terms.

PSI clue: Principal stays; interest is paid.

22

Fixed-period settlement

Proceeds are paid in installments for a specified period; the payment amount is determined by the proceeds, interest, and chosen time period.

PSI clue: HOW LONG is fixed.

23

Fixed-amount settlement

A specified dollar amount is paid periodically until proceeds and interest are exhausted.

PSI clue: HOW MUCH is fixed.

24

Life-income settlement

Payments continue for the beneficiary's lifetime; payment amount depends on factors such as age and option selected.

PSI clue: FOR LIFE.

25

Joint-and-survivor income

Income continues while either of two annuitants/beneficiaries remains alive under the selected option.

PSI clue: Pays until the second of two lives ends.

26

Cash surrender

The owner terminates the permanent policy and receives available cash surrender value, ending the death protection.

PSI clue: Take cash; coverage ends.

27

Extended term

Cash value purchases term insurance for the original face amount for as long a period as the value will buy.

PSI clue: SAME face amount, TEMPORARY.

28

Reduced paid-up

Cash value purchases a smaller amount of fully paid permanent insurance with no further premiums required.

PSI clue: SMALLER face amount, PERMANENT, no more premiums.

29

Policy loan

A permanent policyowner may borrow against available cash value; unpaid loan balance and interest reduce policy values/death proceeds and can cause lapse if excessive.

PSI clue: Borrow from policy value; unpaid debt reduces benefit.

30

Automatic premium loan

If elected/provided and sufficient cash value exists, the insurer automatically loans policy value to pay an overdue premium and help prevent lapse.

PSI clue: Cash value pays overdue premium as a loan.

31

Withdrawal / partial surrender

Certain permanent policies allow withdrawals that reduce account/cash value and may reduce death benefit; tax consequences depend on policy status and basis.

PSI clue: Take value out without full surrender.

32

Dividend — cash

A declared dividend may be taken in cash.

PSI clue: Dividend paid directly.

33

Dividend — reduce premium

A declared dividend may be applied toward the premium due.

PSI clue: Dividend lowers premium payment.

34

Dividend — accumulate at interest

A declared dividend may remain with the insurer and earn interest; the interest is generally taxable when received or credited under tax rules.

PSI clue: Dividend stays; earns interest.

35

Dividend — one-year term

A declared dividend may purchase one-year term insurance, often increasing temporary death protection.

PSI clue: Dividend buys temporary extra coverage.

36

Dividend — paid-up additions

A declared dividend may purchase additional fully paid permanent insurance, increasing cash value and death benefit.

PSI clue: Dividend buys little chunks of permanent insurance.

37

Waiver of premium

A waiver-of-premium rider excuses required premiums while a qualifying total disability continues after any elimination period and subject to rider terms.

PSI clue: Disabled as defined → premiums waived.

38

Waiver of cost of insurance

On certain universal life policies, this rider may waive monthly mortality/cost-of-insurance charges during qualifying disability rather than waiving a fixed premium.

PSI clue: UL rider can waive COI charges.

39

Disability income rider

A disability income rider can provide a specified income benefit if the insured meets the rider's disability definition.

PSI clue: Life policy rider pays disability income.

40

Payor benefit

On juvenile insurance, the payor benefit rider can waive premiums if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined while the child is still within the rider's covered period.

PSI clue: Protects child's policy if payor cannot pay.

41

Accelerated death benefit

An accelerated benefit permits early access to part of the death benefit upon qualifying conditions such as terminal or specified severe illness, reducing the remaining death benefit.

PSI clue: Living access to death benefit; remainder goes down.

42

Long-term-care rider

A long-term-care rider can accelerate policy benefits when the insured meets contractual LTC eligibility conditions, commonly based on functional or cognitive impairment.

PSI clue: Use life benefits for qualifying LTC needs.

43

Spouse/other-insured term rider

Adds term coverage on a spouse or another insured to the base policy.

PSI clue: Term coverage for another person.

44

Children's term rider

Provides term insurance on eligible children under the base policy, commonly convertible under rider terms.

PSI clue: One rider can cover eligible children.

45

Family term rider

Combines term coverage for specified family members under the base insured's policy.

PSI clue: Family members get term coverage.

46

Accidental death rider

Pays an additional amount if death meets the rider's definition of qualifying accidental death and exclusions do not apply.

PSI clue: Qualifying accident can increase death benefit.

47

Guaranteed insurability

Allows the insured to purchase specified additional coverage at certain times without new evidence of insurability, subject to rider limits.

PSI clue: Future insurance option without new medical proof.

48

Cost-of-living rider

Can increase coverage according to a stated inflation-related measure or formula, subject to rider terms.

PSI clue: Designed to help coverage keep pace with inflation.

49

Return-of-premium rider

Provides a return of specified premiums under the rider's terms, often if the insured survives a stated term; it is not the same as cash value from whole life.

PSI clue: Term-style refund feature under stated conditions.

6 scored questions

Annuities

6%

Focus on parties, accumulation vs payout, immediate vs deferred, fixed vs variable/indexed, surrender features, and lifetime-income options.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Accumulation period vs annuity period; owner, annuitant, beneficiary; insurance aspects of annuities
  • Immediate vs deferred; SPIA; deferred premiums; nonforfeiture; surrender/withdrawal charges; death benefits
  • Fixed annuities: general account, minimum/current interest guarantees, level benefit payments
  • Indexed annuities and market-value-adjusted annuities
  • Uses: lump-sum settlements, qualified retirement plans, group vs individual, IRAs, tax-deferred growth, retirement income, education funding

Complete concept guide

1

Accumulation period

The accumulation period is when money goes into the annuity and value grows before annuitized income begins.

PSI clue: Money IN / growing.

2

Annuity period

The annuity or payout period begins when accumulated value is converted into periodic income payments.

PSI clue: Money OUT / income.

3

Annuity owner

The owner controls contractual rights such as beneficiary changes, permitted withdrawals, surrender, or annuitization elections.

PSI clue: Owner controls the contract.

4

Annuitant

The annuitant is the measuring life used to determine life-contingent annuity benefits; the annuitant does not automatically own the contract.

PSI clue: Life expectancy person.

5

Annuity beneficiary

The beneficiary is designated to receive applicable death benefits or remaining values under contract terms.

PSI clue: Receives death-related annuity value.

6

Immediate annuity

An immediate annuity begins income payments soon after purchase, generally within one payment interval and no later than about one year depending on contract convention.

PSI clue: Income starts soon.

7

Deferred annuity

A deferred annuity has an accumulation period before income payments begin.

PSI clue: Grow first, pay later.

8

SPIA

A single-premium immediate annuity is funded with one premium and begins payments shortly thereafter.

PSI clue: One deposit; income starts soon.

9

Flexible-premium deferred annuity

A deferred annuity may accept multiple premiums over time rather than a single premium.

PSI clue: Multiple contributions before payout.

10

Surrender charge

A surrender charge is a contract charge that may apply to withdrawals or surrender during an early contract period.

PSI clue: Early exit can cost a charge.

11

Annuity death benefit

Before annuitization, many annuities provide a contractual death benefit to the beneficiary based on account value or a stated formula.

PSI clue: Pre-annuitization death can trigger beneficiary value.

12

Fixed annuity

A fixed annuity is backed by the insurer's general account; the insurer bears investment risk and guarantees interest/values as stated in the contract.

PSI clue: FIXED = insurer risk.

13

Variable annuity

A variable annuity uses separate-account investment options; the owner bears investment risk and contract value/payments can fluctuate.

PSI clue: VARIABLE = owner risk.

14

Indexed annuity

An indexed annuity credits interest using a formula linked to an external index but is an insurance contract, not direct ownership of the index.

PSI clue: Index-linked interest with contract limits.

15

Market value adjusted annuity

A market value adjustment can increase or decrease certain surrender/withdrawal values based on changes in market interest rates under contract terms.

PSI clue: Interest-rate movement can alter surrender value.

16

Straight life annuity

Payments continue for the annuitant's lifetime and generally stop at death, producing the highest life-only income among comparable options because no refund/guarantee period is built in.

PSI clue: Lifetime only; stops at death.

17

Life with period certain

Pays for life, but guarantees payments for at least a stated period; if the annuitant dies early, the beneficiary receives remaining guaranteed payments.

PSI clue: Life + minimum guaranteed years.

18

Joint and survivor annuity

Pays while either of two annuitants remains alive, usually at a stated continuation percentage.

PSI clue: Two lives; continues to survivor.

19

Longevity risk

Annuities are designed primarily to address the risk of outliving assets by converting value into income that can last for life.

PSI clue: Risk of living too long for your money.

20

Tax-deferred growth

Annuity earnings generally grow tax-deferred while they remain inside the contract; tax-deferred does not mean tax-free.

PSI clue: Tax later, not never.

5 scored questions

Federal Tax Considerations

5%
2026 IRS update worth knowing: The combined Traditional/Roth IRA contribution limit is $7,500 for 2026; the age-50+ IRA catch-up is $1,100. Current RMD rules use age 73 for people who attain age 73 before 2033; age 75 applies to later cohorts under SECURE 2.0. Licensing exams often emphasize the concepts more than annual dollar limits, so prioritize tax-deferred vs tax-free, basis vs gain, early-distribution rules, rollovers/transfers, and 1035 exchanges.

Know the exam-level tax treatment of life proceeds, cash value, dividends, loans/surrenders, nonqualified annuities, IRAs, rollovers, and 1035 exchanges. Tax-deferred never means tax-free.

What PSI can test in this domain

  • Personal life insurance: cash value increases, dividends, loans, surrenders, amounts received by beneficiary, settlement options
  • Nonqualified annuities: accumulation withdrawals, annuity phase/exclusion ratio, distributions at death, corporate-owned issues
  • Traditional IRAs: contributions/deductibility, premature distributions, annuity phase, beneficiary amounts
  • Roth IRAs: contributions/limits and distributions
  • Rollovers and transfers; Section 1035 exchanges; qualified-plan requirements and age-related distribution taxation

Complete concept guide

1

Life cash value growth

Cash value growth inside a life insurance policy is generally tax-deferred while it remains in the policy.

PSI clue: Cash value grows without annual current tax in the usual case.

2

Life policy dividends

Life policy dividends are generally treated as a return of premium to the extent they do not exceed the policyowner's cost basis; interest earned on retained dividends is taxable.

PSI clue: Dividend itself often return of premium; dividend interest taxable.

3

Policy loans

A life insurance policy loan is generally not taxable when taken if the contract remains in force and is not subject to special rules, but a lapse or surrender with an outstanding loan can create taxable gain.

PSI clue: Loan usually not current income; lapse/surrender can change that.

4

Life surrender

When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.

PSI clue: Surrender gain = taxable.

5

Death benefit

Life insurance death proceeds paid by reason of the insured's death are generally excluded from the beneficiary's gross income, subject to exceptions such as transfer-for-value rules.

PSI clue: Death benefit generally income-tax-free.

6

Interest on death proceeds

Interest paid on retained or delayed life insurance proceeds is taxable interest income even though the underlying death benefit may be income-tax-free.

PSI clue: Death benefit free; INTEREST taxable.

7

Nonqualified annuity accumulation

Earnings in a nonqualified annuity generally grow tax-deferred during accumulation.

PSI clue: No annual tax on inside buildup in the usual case.

8

Nonqualified annuity withdrawal — LIFO

Before annuitization, withdrawals from a nonqualified annuity with gain are generally treated as earnings first (LIFO) until gain is exhausted; taxable earnings are ordinary income.

PSI clue: GAIN comes out first.

9

Annuity exclusion ratio

During annuitization of a nonqualified contract, each payment may contain a tax-free return of investment and a taxable earnings portion determined under applicable tax rules/exclusion ratio.

PSI clue: Annuitized payment can be part basis, part taxable earnings.

10

Annuity early-distribution tax

Taxable distributions from nonqualified annuities before age 59½ are generally subject to an additional 10% federal tax unless an exception applies.

PSI clue: Before 59½ can add 10% tax on taxable part.

11

Traditional IRA

Traditional IRA contributions may be deductible depending on circumstances; earnings generally grow tax-deferred and taxable distributions are generally ordinary income.

PSI clue: Possible deduction now; tax generally later.

12

Roth IRA

Roth IRA contributions are made with after-tax dollars; qualified distributions are generally tax-free when requirements are met.

PSI clue: After-tax contribution; qualified payout tax-free.

13

IRA early distributions

Taxable IRA distributions before age 59½ are generally subject to a 10% additional federal tax unless an exception applies.

PSI clue: 59½ is the common early-distribution line.

14

60-day rollover

A qualifying rollover generally must be completed within 60 days when the participant receives the distribution, unless an exception/waiver applies; direct trustee-to-trustee transfers avoid this receipt issue.

PSI clue: Rollover after receiving funds: think 60 days.

15

Trustee-to-trustee transfer

A direct transfer from one IRA trustee to another is not treated as a rollover distribution to the owner and is generally tax-free.

PSI clue: Money moves trustee to trustee; owner never takes possession.

16

1035 exchange

Internal Revenue Code Section 1035 can permit certain exchanges of life insurance, endowment, or annuity contracts without current recognition of gain when statutory requirements are met.

PSI clue: Qualified contract-for-contract exchange can defer gain.

17

Qualified retirement plan

A qualified plan meets Internal Revenue Code requirements and receives tax advantages; distributions are taxed according to the plan and contribution type.

PSI clue: Meets tax-code requirements for favorable treatment.

No orphan bullets

9. Official-outline microtopics — explicitly taught

These are public-outline bullets that are easy for study guides to bury inside broader chapters. They are printed separately here so nothing is only implied.

Insurance Regulation

Producer duties

A producer must be properly licensed for activities requiring a license, act within authority, handle premiums fiduciarly, use truthful sales practices, and comply with Michigan insurance law and insurer procedures.

Insurance Regulation

Producer vs counselor vs business entity

A producer sells/solicits/negotiates insurance. A counselor provides insurance advice for compensation under the counselor license. A business entity such as a corporation/LLC/partnership acting as a producer must itself be licensed.

Insurance Regulation

Assumed name / DBA

A licensee using an assumed business name must comply with Michigan registration/licensing requirements; exam questions usually test that the regulator must be able to identify the true licensed person/entity.

Insurance Regulation

Penalties and fines

The Director may use administrative remedies such as cease-and-desist orders, probation, suspension, revocation/refusal, and statutory fines depending on the violation.

Insurance Regulation

Illegal inducement

An illegal inducement is a benefit offered to persuade a purchase when the benefit is not permitted by the policy/law. Rebating is the classic exam example.

General Insurance

Loss

A loss is an unintended reduction in economic value. Insurance is designed to transfer or finance qualifying loss exposures, not create a profit opportunity.

General Insurance

Producer–insurer relationship

An appointed producer acts as the insurer's agent within granted authority. The insurer can be bound by authorized producer acts; apparent authority can arise from the insurer's conduct toward third parties.

General Insurance

Responsibility to applicant/insured

A producer should present coverage accurately, obtain complete information, submit applications/premiums properly, explain relevant policy features, and avoid misleading or unfair practices.

General Insurance

Indemnity concept

Indemnity means restoring an insured to approximately the pre-loss financial position rather than allowing profit. Life insurance is a valued contract and does not operate as strict indemnity because a human life cannot be measured after death like property damage.

Life Insurance Basics

Security as a personal use

Life insurance can provide financial security by creating funds when death would otherwise disrupt a family's or business's finances.

Life Insurance Basics

Information gathered in a needs analysis

Typical facts include income, debts, mortgage, dependents, existing insurance/assets, final expenses, education goals, survivor income needs, and available liquid resources.

Life Insurance Basics

Lump-sum needs

Needs analysis first identifies immediate capital needs such as final expenses, emergency funds, debt/mortgage payoff, education funding, and other one-time obligations.

Life Insurance Basics

Income needs

After lump-sum needs, estimate ongoing survivor income needs and how long income is required, then offset by available resources and expected income.

Life Insurance Basics

Policy review

At delivery, the producer should review benefits, exclusions, riders, premiums, beneficiary/owner information, and other important provisions so the owner understands the contract.

Life Insurance Basics

Effective date of coverage

Coverage begins according to the policy/receipt rules. When no premium was paid with the application, coverage commonly does not begin until delivery, required premium payment, and any required statement of continued good health.

Life Insurance Basics

Premium collection

The producer must handle collected premiums as fiduciary funds and issue/handle receipts according to insurer procedures. A conditional receipt can create conditional coverage when its stated requirements are satisfied.

Life Insurance Policies

Level-premium term

Level-premium term uses a premium designed to remain level for the stated term even though mortality cost rises with age; the death benefit is commonly level for that term.

Life Insurance Policies

Flexible-premium policy family

Flexible-premium policies allow more owner control over premium timing/amount than traditional whole life. Universal life is the major exam example.

Policy Provisions, Options, Riders

Payment of premiums provision

The policy states premium amount/mode and when payment is due. Premium mode can be annual, semiannual, quarterly, or monthly; more frequent modes usually produce a higher total annual cost.

Policy Provisions, Options, Riders

Minor beneficiary

An insurer may be unable to pay a large death benefit directly to a minor; a guardian, custodian, trustee, or other lawful arrangement may be needed.

Policy Provisions, Options, Riders

Trust as beneficiary

Naming a trust can place proceeds under trustee control and distribute them according to the trust terms.

Policy Provisions, Options, Riders

Beneficiary succession

Primary beneficiaries are first in line; contingent beneficiaries receive proceeds if the primary beneficiary cannot receive them under the contract.

Policy Provisions, Options, Riders

Single-life income settlement

Payments continue for one payee's lifetime. It usually maximizes lifetime income compared with options that add guarantees because payments stop at that person's death.

Annuities

Insurance aspect / longevity risk

The defining insurance function of an annuity is protection against outliving income. Life-contingent payout options pool longevity risk among annuitants.

Annuities

Premium-payment options

An annuity can be funded by one premium or flexible/periodic premiums depending on contract type. 'Single premium' describes funding, while 'immediate/deferred' describes when income begins.

Annuities

Annuity nonforfeiture

A deferred annuity generally has a value the owner can receive on surrender subject to contract terms, surrender charges, and applicable minimum-value rules.

Annuities

General-account assets

Fixed annuity premiums are generally supported by the insurer's general account; the insurer bears investment risk and promises contract guarantees.

Annuities

Minimum vs current interest

A fixed annuity can credit a current rate above the guaranteed minimum. The current rate may change; the guaranteed minimum is the contractual floor.

Annuities

Level benefit payment amount

A fixed annuity's fixed payout option provides a stated/level dollar payment under the selected settlement terms; variable-annuity payments can fluctuate.

Annuities

Lump-sum settlement use

An annuity can accept a lump sum and convert it into systematic retirement income; a SPIA is the classic single-premium, near-immediate-income design.

Annuities

Group vs individual annuity

Individual annuities are owned directly by an individual. Group annuity contracts can fund employer or association retirement arrangements with participants receiving certificates/benefits.

Annuities

Education-fund use

Tax-deferred annuity accumulation can be used toward future education expenses, but annuities do not become tax-free merely because the money is used for education.

Federal Tax

Life settlement-option tax treatment

The death benefit itself is generally excluded from federal income tax, but interest earned or separately paid on retained proceeds is generally taxable.

Federal Tax

Nonqualified annuity distributions at death

Death of an owner/annuitant can trigger contract distribution rules; taxable gain is not magically erased. Beneficiary taxation depends on contract structure and payout method.

Federal Tax

Corporate-owned life/annuity caution

Business-owned contracts can have special federal tax, notice/consent, and reporting rules. For the producer exam, know that ownership purpose and tax treatment can differ from personally owned coverage.

Federal Tax

Traditional IRA contributions and deductibility

A traditional IRA contribution may be deductible, partially deductible, or nondeductible depending on income and workplace-plan coverage. The 2026 combined traditional/Roth IRA contribution limit is $7,500, with a $1,100 age-50+ catch-up.

Federal Tax

Traditional IRA annuity-phase payments

Taxation depends on whether the IRA contains any basis. Deductible contributions and earnings are generally taxable when distributed; nondeductible basis is recovered tax-free under IRS rules.

Federal Tax

IRA beneficiary amounts

Inherited IRA distribution rules depend on beneficiary type and timing. For exam purposes, distinguish beneficiary distributions from the owner's normal lifetime distribution rules.

Federal Tax

Roth IRA contributions / limits

Roth contributions are made with after-tax dollars and are not deductible. Eligibility can be limited by income. Qualified Roth distributions are generally tax-free.

Federal Tax

Qualified-plan general requirements

Qualified plans receive favorable tax treatment by meeting Internal Revenue Code requirements; contributions/earnings commonly receive tax deferral until distribution.

Federal Tax

Age-related distribution taxation

Early taxable retirement distributions generally can face an additional tax before age 59½ unless an exception applies. Current RMD starting age depends on birth year; under current law many individuals beginning RMDs now use age 73, with age 75 applying to later birth cohorts.

10. Complete practice bank — answers included

All 440 stored practice variants are visible below with the answer and explanation. Use this as a study bank; for a true readiness test, cover the answer box and solve first.

Do not memorize the sentence. For each miss, explain why the correct answer beats the three distractors. That is what transfers when PSI changes wording.

Insurance Regulation — 55 practice variants

#1. Agency contract or first application starts a 15-day filing clock. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Producer appointment — 15 days
  2. B. Appointment eligibility — 30 days
  3. C. Ineligible appointment notice — 5 days
  4. D. Termination reporting — 30 days
Answer: A. Producer appointment — 15 days
Why: An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.
#2. Which statement BEST describes Producer appointment — 15 days?
  1. A. An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.
  2. B. If the producer is found ineligible for appointment, the director notifies the insurer within 5 days of that determination.
  3. C. After filing certain termination notices, the insurer sends a copy to the producer within 15 days.
  4. D. A licensee reports a legal name or address change within 30 days.
Answer: A. An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.
Why: Agency contract or first application starts a 15-day filing clock.
#3. DIFS eligibility check: think 30. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Appointment eligibility — 30 days
  2. B. Ineligible appointment notice — 5 days
  3. C. Termination reporting — 30 days
  4. D. Termination copy to producer — 15 days
Answer: A. Appointment eligibility — 30 days
Why: After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.
#4. Which statement BEST describes Appointment eligibility — 30 days?
  1. A. After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.
  2. B. An insurer generally reports termination of a producer business relationship within 30 days after the effective date.
  3. C. A producer may submit written comments concerning a termination notice within 30 days after receiving it.
  4. D. A resident producer generally completes 24 hours of approved continuing education in each 2-year review period.
Answer: A. After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.
Why: DIFS eligibility check: think 30.

Why: Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.
#42. Which statement BEST describes Insurance fraud?
  1. A. Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.
  2. B. The director may order a person to stop conduct that violates insurance law or an applicable order/rule.
  3. C. Commissions for insurance sales are generally paid only as permitted by licensing law; unlicensed persons may not be paid for activities requiring a producer license.
  4. D. After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.

#48. Which statement BEST describes License discipline?

  2. B. An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.

#49. License-required activity → compensation rules matter. Which term or rule BEST matches?

  3. C. Appointment eligibility — 30 days

#50. Which statement BEST describes Commissions?
  1. A. Commissions for insurance sales are generally paid only as permitted by licensing law; unlicensed persons may not be paid for activities requiring a producer license.
  2. B. After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.
  3. C. An insurer generally reports termination of a producer business relationship within 30 days after the effective date.
  4. D. A producer may submit written comments concerning a termination notice within 30 days after receiving it.

Why: License-required activity → compensation rules matter.
#51. An insurer executes an agency contract with a producer on June 1. No application has yet been submitted. Under the Michigan appointment rule, the insurer's appointment filing is generally due within:
  1. A. 5 days
  2. B. 15 days
  3. C. 30 days
  4. D. 31 days
Answer: B. 15 days
Why: Appointment notice: 15 days from agency contract execution or first application, whichever event triggers the statutory filing rule.
#52. A producer's administrative action in another jurisdiction reaches final disposition. The producer generally has how long to report it to DIFS?
  1. A. 5 days
  2. B. 10 days
  3. C. 15 days
  4. D. 30 days
Answer: D. 30 days
Why: Covered administrative actions are generally reported within 30 days after final disposition.
#53. A producer deposits an applicant's premium into the producer's personal checking account. The most direct regulatory problem is a violation of:
  1. A. Fiduciary responsibility
  2. B. Apparent authority
  3. C. Insurable interest
  4. D. The law of large numbers
Answer: A. Fiduciary responsibility
Why: Premium money is entrusted money; handling it for personal use violates fiduciary duties.
#54. A producer gives a prospect a $100 gift card from the producer's commission only if the prospect buys a policy, and the benefit is not in the contract. This most directly describes:
  1. A. Twisting
  2. B. Rebating
  3. C. Defamation
  4. D. Estoppel
Answer: B. Rebating
Why: An unlisted valuable inducement tied to buying insurance is a classic rebating/illegal-inducement issue.
#55. A producer uses false comparisons about a client's existing policy specifically to induce replacement. This is:
  1. A. Twisting
  2. B. Rebating
  3. C. Waiver
  4. D. Adverse selection
Answer: A. Twisting
Why: Misleading replacement is twisting.

General Insurance — 65 practice variants

#56. Uncertainty of loss = risk. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Risk
  2. B. Pure risk
  3. C. Speculative risk
  4. D. Avoidance
Answer: A. Risk
Why: Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
#57. Which statement BEST describes Risk?
  1. A. Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
  2. B. Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
  3. C. Risk retention means keeping responsibility for some or all of a potential loss.
  4. D. Risk transfer shifts the financial consequences of loss to another party; insurance is a classic method.
Answer: A. Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
Why: Uncertainty of loss = risk.
#58. Loss or no loss; no upside. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Pure risk
  2. B. Speculative risk
  3. C. Avoidance
  4. D. Retention
Answer: A. Pure risk
Why: Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
#59. Which statement BEST describes Pure risk?
  1. A. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
  2. B. Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
  3. C. Risk sharing distributes potential loss among multiple parties.
  4. D. An insurable risk is generally definite, measurable, accidental from the insured's standpoint, economically feasible, and part of a large enough pool for prediction.
Answer: A. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
Why: Loss or no loss; no upside.
#60. Gain is possible = speculative. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Speculative risk
  2. B. Avoidance
  3. C. Retention
  4. D. Sharing

Why: Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.

  3. C. Risk transfer shifts the financial consequences of loss to another party; insurance is a classic method.
  4. D. Adverse selection is the tendency of people with greater-than-average risk to seek or keep insurance more readily.
Answer: A. Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
Why: Gain is possible = speculative.
