  2. B. Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
  3. C. Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
  4. D. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
Answer: A. A warranty is a statement or promise that is guaranteed to be literally true; insurance applications usually treat statements as representations instead.
Why: Absolute guarantee of truth = warranty.
#108. Material fact intentionally hidden. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Concealment
  2. B. Fraud
  3. C. Waiver
  4. D. Estoppel
Answer: A. Concealment
Why: Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.
#109. Which statement BEST describes Concealment?
  1. A. Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.
  2. B. Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
  3. C. Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
  4. D. Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
Answer: A. Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.
Why: Material fact intentionally hidden.
#110. Intentional deceit for gain. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Fraud
  2. B. Waiver
  3. C. Estoppel
  4. D. Risk
Answer: A. Fraud
Why: Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
#111. Which statement BEST describes Fraud?
  1. A. Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
  2. B. Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
  3. C. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
  4. D. Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
Answer: A. Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
Why: Intentional deceit for gain.
#112. Know the right, choose to give it up. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Waiver
  2. B. Estoppel
  3. C. Risk
  4. D. Pure risk
Answer: A. Waiver
Why: Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
#113. Which statement BEST describes Waiver?
  1. A. Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
  2. B. Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
  3. C. Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
  4. D. Risk retention means keeping responsibility for some or all of a potential loss.
Answer: A. Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
Why: Know the right, choose to give it up.
#114. Prior conduct blocks later inconsistent position. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Estoppel
  2. B. Risk
  3. C. Pure risk
  4. D. Speculative risk
Answer: A. Estoppel
Why: Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
#115. Which statement BEST describes Estoppel?
  1. A. Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
  2. B. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
  3. C. Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
  4. D. Risk sharing distributes potential loss among multiple parties.
Answer: A. Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
Why: Prior conduct blocks later inconsistent position.
#116. A producer's contract expressly authorizes solicitation. Scheduling appointments is not mentioned but is reasonably necessary to solicit. Scheduling appointments is supported by:
  1. A. Express authority
  2. B. Implied authority
  3. C. Apparent authority
  4. D. Fiduciary authority
Answer: B. Implied authority
Why: Not stated but necessary to perform express duties = implied authority.
#117. An insurer gives a producer company-branded forms and identification that lead a customer reasonably to believe the producer is authorized. This is:
  1. A. Express authority
  2. B. Implied authority
  3. C. Apparent authority
  4. D. Waiver
Answer: C. Apparent authority
Why: Apparent authority comes from the insurer's manifestations to a third party.
#118. A homeowner faces the possibility of a house fire. There is no chance of financial gain from whether the fire occurs. This is:
  1. A. Speculative risk
  2. B. Pure risk
  3. C. Moral hazard
  4. D. Adverse selection
Answer: B. Pure risk
Why: Pure risk = loss or no loss, not gain.
#119. An insurance contract is called aleatory because:
  1. A. The insurer drafts the language
  2. B. Only the insurer makes an enforceable promise
  3. C. Values exchanged may be unequal depending on an uncertain event
  4. D. It always restores an insured to the exact pre-loss financial position
Answer: C. Values exchanged may be unequal depending on an uncertain event
Why: Aleatory refers to potentially unequal values tied to uncertainty.
#120. An applicant intentionally leaves out a material fact that the application calls for. This is best described as:
  1. A. Waiver
  2. B. Concealment
  3. C. Estoppel
  4. D. Indemnity
Answer: B. Concealment
Why: Intentional nondisclosure of a material fact is concealment.

Life Insurance Basics — 82 practice variants

#121. Life insurable interest is tested at policy inception. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Insurable interest
  2. B. Variable product regulation
  3. C. Solicitation and sales presentations
  4. D. Life insurance advertising
Answer: A. Insurable interest
Why: For life insurance, insurable interest must generally exist when the policy is issued; the policyowner must face a genuine financial or close-family interest in the insured's continued life.
#122. Which statement BEST describes Insurable interest?
  1. A. For life insurance, insurable interest must generally exist when the policy is issued; the policyowner must face a genuine financial or close-family interest in the insured's continued life.
  2. B. Sales presentations must be truthful and not misleading, and required disclosures must be provided.
  3. C. The guaranty association is a safety-net mechanism for covered obligations of insolvent member insurers, subject to statutory limits; it is not a sales guarantee or reason to recommend a policy.
  4. D. A policy summary presents key information about the proposed policy in a standardized consumer-oriented form.
Answer: A. For life insurance, insurable interest must generally exist when the policy is issued; the policyowner must face a genuine financial or close-family interest in the insured's continued life.
Why: Life insurable interest is tested at policy inception.

Answer: A. Premium mode is the frequency of payment, such as annual, semiannual, quarterly, or monthly; more frequent modes can produce a higher total annual outlay.
Why: Mode = how often premium is paid.

#197. A business buys life insurance on its chief engineer because the engineer's death would cause the business a significant financial loss. The business owns the policy and is beneficiary. This is:
  1. A. Credit life
  2. B. Key person life
  3. C. Group conversion
  4. D. Buyer's guide coverage
Answer: B. Key person life
Why: Key person insurance protects the business against financial loss from the death of a key employee.
#198. Partners use life insurance so surviving owners can buy a deceased owner's interest. The insurance is funding a:
  1. A. Key person plan
  2. B. Buy-sell agreement

#200. An underwriter sees an MIB code inconsistent with the application. What is the best next conclusion?

  2. B. The MIB has made the underwriting decision

  4. D. The applicant automatically committed fraud
Answer: C. The insurer should investigate the discrepancy further
Why: MIB information is an alert/information source; the insurer still investigates and makes the decision.
#201. An applicant has lower-than-average expected mortality and excellent underwriting characteristics. The most likely risk class is:
  1. A. Preferred
  2. B. Standard
  3. C. Substandard
  4. D. Declined
Answer: A. Preferred
Why: Preferred = better-than-average mortality risk.
#202. Which method of estimating life-insurance need starts by listing specific survivor obligations and income needs, then subtracting available assets?
  1. A. Human life value approach
  2. B. Needs approach
  3. C. Law of large numbers
  4. D. Capital retention only
Answer: B. Needs approach
Why: Needs approach focuses on actual needs and resources.

Life Insurance Policies — 45 practice variants

#203. Term coverage with level face amount. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Level term
  2. B. Renewable term
  3. C. Convertible term
  4. D. Decreasing term
Answer: A. Level term
Why: Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.
#204. Which statement BEST describes Level term?
  1. A. Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.
  2. B. Convertible term permits conversion to a permanent policy without new evidence of insurability, within contract limits.
  3. C. Straight life whole life uses level premiums payable for life and provides permanent death protection with guaranteed cash value according to the contract.
  4. D. Single-premium whole life is funded by one large premium and is then paid-up, subject to tax rules such as MEC treatment if applicable.
Answer: A. Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.
Why: Term coverage with level face amount.

  3. C. Limited-pay whole life becomes paid-up after premiums are paid for a specified shorter period while coverage continues for life.
  4. D. Graded premium policies start with lower premiums that increase on a schedule before leveling at a higher amount.

  3. C. Credit life is designed to pay or reduce a covered borrower's debt if the insured borrower dies.

Answer: A. Universal life is flexible-premium permanent insurance with an account value, mortality/expense charges, and an adjustable death benefit subject to contract rules.
Why: Flexible premium + adjustable death benefit + account value.

#248. Owner controls the contract. Which term or rule BEST matches?

  3. C. Collateral assignment
  4. D. Entire contract

  3. C. Policy changes generally must meet the contract's modification requirements and be made by an authorized officer rather than informally by a producer.
  4. D. After the first policy year, Michigan individual life policies generally include a 1-month grace period for premium payment while coverage continues, subject to statutory terms.

Why: Provides a return of specified premiums under the rider's terms, often if the insured survives a stated term; it is not the same as cash value from whole life.
#345. Which statement BEST describes Return-of-premium rider?
  1. A. Provides a return of specified premiums under the rider's terms, often if the insured survives a stated term; it is not the same as cash value from whole life.
  2. B. Absolute assignment transfers all ownership rights in the policy to another party.
  3. C. The policy together with the attached application and applicable riders/endorsements constitutes the entire contract.
  4. D. Michigan individual life policies generally provide at least 10 days after receipt to return the policy for cancellation and refund under the statutory right-to-examine rule.
Answer: A. Provides a return of specified premiums under the rider's terms, often if the insured survives a stated term; it is not the same as cash value from whole life.
Why: Term-style refund feature under stated conditions.
#346. A whole-life owner stops paying premiums but wants the original face amount to continue for as long as existing cash value can buy it. Choose the nonforfeiture option:
  1. A. Reduced paid-up
  2. B. Extended term
  3. C. Cash surrender
  4. D. Automatic premium loan
Answer: B. Extended term
Why: Extended term keeps the original face amount but only temporarily.
#347. A whole-life owner stops paying premiums and wants a smaller amount of permanent coverage with no further premiums. Choose:
  1. A. Extended term
  2. B. Reduced paid-up
  3. C. Cash surrender
  4. D. Reinstatement
Answer: B. Reduced paid-up
Why: Reduced paid-up = smaller permanent coverage, no more premiums.
#348. A beneficiary receives $2,500 each month until policy proceeds and interest are exhausted. Which settlement option is this?
  1. A. Fixed period
  2. B. Fixed amount
  3. C. Life income
  4. D. Interest only
Answer: B. Fixed amount
Why: The dollar amount is fixed.
#349. A beneficiary receives equal installments for exactly 15 years. Which settlement option is this?
  1. A. Fixed amount
  2. B. Fixed period
  3. C. Life income
  4. D. Cash
Answer: B. Fixed period
Why: The period is fixed.
#350. A policyowner transfers all ownership rights to another person. This is:
  1. A. Collateral assignment
  2. B. Absolute assignment
  3. C. Irrevocable designation
  4. D. Replacement
Answer: B. Absolute assignment
Why: Absolute assignment transfers all ownership rights.
#351. A bank receives policy rights only to secure an outstanding loan. This is:
  1. A. Absolute assignment
  2. B. Collateral assignment
  3. C. Revocable beneficiary designation
  4. D. Paid-up addition
Answer: B. Collateral assignment
Why: Collateral assignment secures debt and is limited to the creditor's interest.
#352. A policyowner names an irrevocable beneficiary and later wants to change that beneficiary. What is generally required?
  1. A. No consent
  2. B. Consent of the irrevocable beneficiary
  3. C. Only producer approval
  4. D. A new policy
Answer: B. Consent of the irrevocable beneficiary
Why: Irrevocable beneficiary rights generally require consent to affected changes.
#353. A declared dividend is used to purchase a small additional amount of fully paid permanent insurance. This option is:
  1. A. One-year term
  2. B. Paid-up additions
  3. C. Accumulation at interest
  4. D. Premium reduction
Answer: B. Paid-up additions
Why: Paid-up additions buy additional permanent insurance.
#354. A qualifying total disability causes future premiums to be excused under a rider. The rider is:
  1. A. Guaranteed insurability
  2. B. Waiver of premium
  3. C. Return of premium
  4. D. Accidental death
Answer: B. Waiver of premium
Why: Waiver of premium protects the policy during qualifying disability.
#355. Which rider allows specified additional insurance purchases at certain future times without new evidence of insurability?
  1. A. Waiver of premium
  2. B. Guaranteed insurability
  3. C. Payor benefit
  4. D. Cost of living
Answer: B. Guaranteed insurability
Why: Guaranteed insurability gives future purchase options without new medical proof.
#356. A $250,000 policy has an $18,000 policy loan and $2,000 unpaid loan interest at death. Ignoring other adjustments, the beneficiary receives:
  1. A. $230,000
  2. B. $232,000
  3. C. $248,000
  4. D. $250,000
Answer: A. $230,000
Why: $250,000 − $18,000 − $2,000 = $230,000.

Federal Tax Considerations — 39 practice variants

#402. Cash value grows without annual current tax in the usual case. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Life cash value growth
  2. B. Life policy dividends
  3. C. Policy loans
  4. D. Life surrender
Answer: A. Life cash value growth
Why: Cash value growth inside a life insurance policy is generally tax-deferred while it remains in the policy.
#403. Which statement BEST describes Life cash value growth?
  1. A. Cash value growth inside a life insurance policy is generally tax-deferred while it remains in the policy.
  2. B. A life insurance policy loan is generally not taxable when taken if the contract remains in force and is not subject to special rules, but a lapse or surrender with an outstanding loan can create taxable gain.
  3. C. Life insurance death proceeds paid by reason of the insured's death are generally excluded from the beneficiary's gross income, subject to exceptions such as transfer-for-value rules.
  4. D. Earnings in a nonqualified annuity generally grow tax-deferred during accumulation.
Answer: A. Cash value growth inside a life insurance policy is generally tax-deferred while it remains in the policy.
Why: Cash value grows without annual current tax in the usual case.
#404. Dividend itself often return of premium; dividend interest taxable. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Life policy dividends
  2. B. Policy loans
  3. C. Life surrender
  4. D. Death benefit
Answer: A. Life policy dividends
Why: Life policy dividends are generally treated as a return of premium to the extent they do not exceed the policyowner's cost basis; interest earned on retained dividends is taxable.
#405. Which statement BEST describes Life policy dividends?
  1. A. Life policy dividends are generally treated as a return of premium to the extent they do not exceed the policyowner's cost basis; interest earned on retained dividends is taxable.
  2. B. When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.
  3. C. Interest paid on retained or delayed life insurance proceeds is taxable interest income even though the underlying death benefit may be income-tax-free.
  4. D. Before annuitization, withdrawals from a nonqualified annuity with gain are generally treated as earnings first (LIFO) until gain is exhausted; taxable earnings are ordinary income.
Answer: A. Life policy dividends are generally treated as a return of premium to the extent they do not exceed the policyowner's cost basis; interest earned on retained dividends is taxable.
Why: Dividend itself often return of premium; dividend interest taxable.
#406. Loan usually not current income; lapse/surrender can change that. Which term or rule BEST matches?

#407. Which statement BEST describes Policy loans?
  1. A. A life insurance policy loan is generally not taxable when taken if the contract remains in force and is not subject to special rules, but a lapse or surrender with an outstanding loan can create taxable gain.
  2. B. Life insurance death proceeds paid by reason of the insured's death are generally excluded from the beneficiary's gross income, subject to exceptions such as transfer-for-value rules.
  3. C. Earnings in a nonqualified annuity generally grow tax-deferred during accumulation.
  4. D. During annuitization of a nonqualified contract, each payment may contain a tax-free return of investment and a taxable earnings portion determined under applicable tax rules/exclusion ratio.
Answer: A. A life insurance policy loan is generally not taxable when taken if the contract remains in force and is not subject to special rules, but a lapse or surrender with an outstanding loan can create taxable gain.
Why: Loan usually not current income; lapse/surrender can change that.
#408. Surrender gain = taxable. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  1. A. Life surrender
  2. B. Death benefit
  3. C. Interest on death proceeds
  4. D. Nonqualified annuity accumulation
Answer: A. Life surrender
Why: When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.
#409. Which statement BEST describes Life surrender?
  1. A. When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.
  2. B. Interest paid on retained or delayed life insurance proceeds is taxable interest income even though the underlying death benefit may be income-tax-free.
  3. C. Before annuitization, withdrawals from a nonqualified annuity with gain are generally treated as earnings first (LIFO) until gain is exhausted; taxable earnings are ordinary income.
  4. D. Taxable distributions from nonqualified annuities before age 59½ are generally subject to an additional 10% federal tax unless an exception applies.
Answer: A. When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.
Why: Surrender gain = taxable.

#436. A beneficiary receives a life insurance death benefit in a lump sum. Under the general federal rule, the death proceeds are:
  1. A. Taxable as ordinary income
  2. B. Generally excluded from gross income
  3. C. Taxed as capital gain
  4. D. Always subject to a 10% penalty
Answer: B. Generally excluded from gross income
Why: Life insurance death proceeds are generally income-tax-free to the beneficiary, subject to exceptions.
#437. A beneficiary leaves life proceeds with the insurer and receives interest. The interest is generally:
  1. A. Tax-free because the death benefit was tax-free
  2. B. Taxable interest income
  3. C. A capital gain
  4. D. Tax-free if paid monthly
Answer: B. Taxable interest income
Why: Interest is taxable even though the underlying death benefit may be excluded.
#438. A nonqualified annuity has $20,000 of gain. Before annuitization the owner withdraws $5,000. The general tax ordering rule treats the withdrawal as:
  1. A. Basis first
  2. B. Earnings first
  3. C. Half basis and half gain
  4. D. Tax-free dividend
Answer: B. Earnings first
Why: Nonqualified annuity withdrawals before annuitization are generally LIFO: earnings first.
#439. Which transaction is designed to move IRA money directly from one trustee to another without the owner receiving a distribution?
  1. A. 60-day rollover
  2. B. Trustee-to-trustee transfer
  3. C. Policy loan
  4. D. 1035 exchange
Answer: B. Trustee-to-trustee transfer
Why: A direct trustee-to-trustee transfer avoids receipt by the owner.
#440. A qualifying exchange of one life insurance or annuity contract for another under Internal Revenue Code rules may defer current gain recognition under:
  1. A. Section 1035
  2. B. Section 4012
  3. C. Section 1208a
  4. D. Section 7702 only
Answer: A. Section 1035
Why: Section 1035 governs qualifying insurance/annuity exchanges.
Closed-book readiness

11. Three full 100-question PSI-style mock forms

Each form uses the exact scored-domain weighting: 17 / 18 / 21 / 12 / 21 / 6 / 5. The three forms use different questions. Answer choices are re-ordered so the correct letter pattern is not predictable. Take them closed-book; the answer key and explanations follow each form.

Mock Form 1 — 100 questions

Target: 90+ without notes. Passing benchmark: 72. Strong readiness gate: 90+ twice, every domain ≥85%.

1. Fiduciary = funds held for someone else. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Twisting
  • B. Fiduciary responsibility
  • C. Misrepresentation
  • D. Rebating

  • A. Twisting

  • C. Rebating
  • D. Estoppel

  • D. Misrepresentation

  • C. Nonparticipating policy

Why each answer is right

1. B — Premiums and other money received in a producer's professional role must be handled in trust and not converted to personal use.
2. C — An unlisted valuable inducement tied to buying insurance is a classic rebating/illegal-inducement issue.
3. B — The owner controls contractual rights such as beneficiary changes, permitted withdrawals, surrender, or annuitization elections.
4. C — A producer reports a covered administrative action within 30 days after final disposition.
5. B — Term provides temporary death protection without cash value; permanent coverage is designed to last for life and commonly builds cash value.
6. A — Pure risk = loss or no loss, not gain.
7. C — Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
8. B — Needs approach focuses on actual needs and resources.
9. D — At least 3 of the 24 continuing-education hours must be ethics in insurance.
10. A — Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.
11. D — Making a false or misleading statement about policy terms, benefits, or advantages to induce a transaction is prohibited.
12. C — Premium money is entrusted money; handling it for personal use violates fiduciary duties.
13. C — The human life value approach estimates the present value of an individual's future economic contribution to dependents.
14. A — Grounds can include material misstatements, fraud, misappropriation, dishonest practices, or violating insurance laws and orders.
15. D — The producer observes and reports relevant applicant information, completes the application accurately, and helps the insurer obtain information; the producer does not make the final underwriting decision.
16. C — Waiver of premium protects the policy during qualifying disability.
17. A — Flexible premiums + adjustable death benefit + account value = universal life.
18. A — A nonparticipating policy does not pay policy dividends.
19. A — The dollar amount is fixed.
20. C — Life with period certain combines lifetime income with a minimum guarantee.
21. B — Group life covers eligible members under a master contract; individual life is issued to a specific policyowner on an individual contract.
22. A — The director may order a person to stop conduct that violates insurance law or an applicable order/rule.
23. D — Life insurance can fund the purchase of a deceased owner's business interest under a buy-sell agreement.
24. A — Knowingly making false statements that damage another insurer's financial condition or reputation is an unfair practice.
25. A — Apparent authority exists when the insurer's actions cause a third party reasonably to believe the producer has authority.
26. B — A stock insurer is owned by shareholders, who provide capital and may receive profits as dividends on stock.
27. D — Absolute assignment transfers all ownership rights.
28. A — The insurer retains the principal and pays interest to the beneficiary according to the settlement terms.
29. A — Convertibility = term to permanent without new evidence.
30. C — MIB information is an alert/information source; the insurer still investigates and makes the decision.
31. A — Appointment notice: 15 days from agency contract execution or first application, whichever event triggers the statutory filing rule.
32. B — Payments continue for the beneficiary's lifetime; payment amount depends on factors such as age and option selected.
33. C — Limited-pay whole life becomes paid-up after premiums are paid for a specified shorter period while coverage continues for life.
34. B — Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.
35. D — Extended term keeps the original face amount but only temporarily.
36. A — Straight life is life-only income.
37. D — APS = Attending Physician Statement.
38. A — Covered administrative actions are generally reported within 30 days after final disposition.
39. D — A producer reports a criminal prosecution within 30 days after the initial pretrial hearing date.
40. B — Life insurance can create an immediate estate by providing a death benefit even if the insured has accumulated few assets.
41. D — Preferred = better-than-average mortality risk.
42. B — Contracting parties must have legal capacity to contract.
43. D — A waiver-of-premium rider excuses required premiums while a qualifying total disability continues after any elimination period and subject to rider terms.
44. B — In group life, the policyholder (often an employer) owns the master contract and covered individuals receive certificates of insurance.
45. A — A business may own and benefit from life insurance on a key employee whose death would cause financial loss.
46. B — One premium + income starts shortly = SPIA.
47. B — A declared dividend may be applied toward the premium due.
48. D — Section 1035 governs qualifying insurance/annuity exchanges.
49. B — Not stated but necessary to perform express duties = implied authority.
50. A — Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
51. C — $250,000 − $18,000 − $2,000 = $230,000.
52. C — An insurance contract is aleatory because the values exchanged may be unequal and depend on an uncertain event.
53. B — Reduced paid-up = smaller permanent coverage, no more premiums.
54. D — After receiving an appointment notice, the director verifies producer eligibility within a reasonable time not exceeding 30 days.
55. C — Fixed annuity investment risk is borne by the insurer.
56. C — Irrevocable beneficiary rights generally require consent to affected changes.
57. C — Adjustable life allows changes to items such as premium, face amount, or coverage period within policy limits, blending term/permanent flexibility.
58. C — Survivorship = second-to-die.
59. D — Intentional nondisclosure of a material fact is concealment.
60. D — Collateral assignment secures debt and is limited to the creditor's interest.
61. A — When qualifying group life coverage terminates, an eligible employee generally has 31 days to apply for an individual policy without new evidence of insurability, subject to statutory terms.
62. D — Group conversion: 31 days.
63. C — After the first policy year, Michigan individual life policies generally include a 1-month grace period for premium payment while coverage continues, subject to statutory terms.
64. C — Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
65. B — A warranty is a statement or promise that is guaranteed to be literally true; insurance applications usually treat statements as representations instead.
66. D — Aleatory refers to potentially unequal values tied to uncertainty.
67. B — Variable life and variable annuity products involve securities regulation in addition to insurance regulation; securities licensing/registration rules can apply.
68. B — A revocable beneficiary can generally be changed by the policyowner without that beneficiary's consent.
69. B — Interest is taxable even though the underlying death benefit may be excluded.
70. B — Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.
71. B — A long-term-care rider can accelerate policy benefits when the insured meets contractual LTC eligibility conditions, commonly based on functional or cognitive impairment.
72. C — Standard applicants present average expected mortality for the insurer's normal rating assumptions.
73. B — Buy-sell funding finances the ownership transfer.
74. B — For life insurance, insurable interest must generally exist when the policy is issued; the policyowner must face a genuine financial or close-family interest in the insured's continued life.
75. C — Life insurance death proceeds are generally income-tax-free to the beneficiary, subject to exceptions.
76. B — Applicants and insureds have protections concerning collection, use, and disclosure of insurance information.
77. A — Apparent authority comes from the insurer's manifestations to a third party.
78. A — Key person insurance protects the business against financial loss from the death of a key employee.
79. B — Life insurance advertising must not create false impressions about benefits, costs, guarantees, or an insurer's identity.
80. D — Insurance is unilateral because only the insurer makes a legally enforceable promise to perform after the insured meets the contract conditions.
81. C — Graded premium policies start with lower premiums that increase on a schedule before leveling at a higher amount.
82. A — A life insurance illustration shows policy values under stated assumptions and must distinguish guaranteed from non-guaranteed elements.
83. D — Offering an inducement or valuable benefit not specified in the contract as an incentive to buy insurance is generally rebating/illegal inducement.
84. B — The period is fixed.
85. C — At delivery, the producer should explain the policy, collect any required premium, and obtain any required statement of continued good health if circumstances require it.
86. D — A declared dividend may remain with the insurer and earn interest; the interest is generally taxable when received or credited under tax rules.
87. A — Borrower + lender + debt = credit life.
88. B — An irrevocable beneficiary has vested rights and generally must consent to changes that affect those rights.
89. B — Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
90. D — Nonqualified annuity withdrawals before annuitization are generally LIFO: earnings first.
91. A — Credit life is designed to pay or reduce a covered borrower's debt if the insured borrower dies.
92. B — Misleading replacement is twisting.
93. C — Guaranteed insurability gives future purchase options without new medical proof.
94. B — A direct trustee-to-trustee transfer avoids receipt by the owner.
95. C — A declared dividend may purchase additional fully paid permanent insurance, increasing cash value and death benefit.
96. D — Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
97. A — Paid-up additions buy additional permanent insurance.
98. A — Risk transfer shifts the financial consequences of loss to another party; insurance is a classic method.
99. B — Variable annuity investment risk belongs to the owner.
100. A — Nonpublic personal and insurance information is subject to privacy, notice, and disclosure restrictions.

Why each answer is right

1. D — Michigan individual life policies generally provide at least 10 days after receipt to return the policy for cancellation and refund under the statutory right-to-examine rule.
2. B — Insurer ends relationship → 30-day report.
3. A — The annuity or payout period begins when accumulated value is converted into periodic income payments.
4. B — The application is a primary source of underwriting information and becomes part of the contract when attached as required.
5. A — In fixed products the insurer generally bears investment risk; in variable products the contract owner bears investment risk through separate-account performance.
6. D — Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
7. D — During annuitization of a nonqualified contract, each payment may contain a tax-free return of investment and a taxable earnings portion determined under applicable tax rules/exclusion ratio.
8. C — Implied authority is not expressly stated but is reasonably necessary to carry out express authority.
9. D — Insurers may not unfairly discriminate between individuals of the same class and equal life expectancy in rates, benefits, or contract terms.
10. D — Adverse selection is the tendency of people with greater-than-average risk to seek or keep insurance more readily.
11. D — An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.
12. D — Survivorship life covers two lives and pays after the second insured dies.
13. D — An accelerated benefit permits early access to part of the death benefit upon qualifying conditions such as terminal or specified severe illness, reducing the remaining death benefit.
14. B — Can increase coverage according to a stated inflation-related measure or formula, subject to rider terms.
15. A — The policyowner controls contractual rights such as naming/changing a revocable beneficiary, assigning the policy, borrowing against cash value, and selecting options, subject to the contract.
16. A — Collateral assignment transfers limited policy rights to a creditor as security for a debt; the assignee's interest is generally limited to the debt.
17. A — A single-premium immediate annuity is funded with one premium and begins payments shortly thereafter.
18. A — Equity-indexed life credits interest using a formula linked to a market index, subject to contract caps, participation rates, floors, and other terms; the policy is not direct ownership of the index.
19. A — An application statement made to the best of the applicant's knowledge is generally treated as a representation rather than an absolute warranty.
20. C — Adds term coverage on a spouse or another insured to the base policy.
21. A — Commissions for insurance sales are generally paid only as permitted by licensing law; unlicensed persons may not be paid for activities requiring a producer license.
22. A — An Attending Physician Statement provides detailed medical information from an applicant's physician when the insurer needs more health detail.
23. C — Forging another person's name on an insurance-related document is grounds for producer discipline.
24. B — Certain permanent policies allow withdrawals that reduce account/cash value and may reduce death benefit; tax consequences depend on policy status and basis.
25. A — Cash value purchases term insurance for the original face amount for as long a period as the value will buy.
26. D — Group life underwriting focuses primarily on the group as a whole rather than full individual evidence of insurability for each member under normal enrollment rules.
27. C — As the number of similar exposure units increases, actual losses tend to become more predictable relative to expected losses.
28. B — The policy together with the attached application and applicable riders/endorsements constitutes the entire contract.
29. C — The guaranty association is a safety-net mechanism for covered obligations of insolvent member insurers, subject to statutory limits; it is not a sales guarantee or reason to recommend a policy.
30. B — The insurer's obligation to perform depends on specified policy conditions being satisfied.
31. C — After filing certain termination notices, the insurer sends a copy to the producer within 15 days.
32. C — If elected/provided and sufficient cash value exists, the insurer automatically loans policy value to pay an overdue premium and help prevent lapse.
33. C — Substandard applicants present higher-than-average expected mortality but may still be insurable with a rating, extra premium, or other terms.
34. B — Using misleading comparisons or misrepresentations to induce a policyowner to lapse, surrender, forfeit, or replace existing coverage is twisting.
35. B — Pays for life, but guarantees payments for at least a stated period; if the annuitant dies early, the beneficiary receives remaining guaranteed payments.
36. C — Life insurance can replace income or provide funds for dependents after the insured's death.
37. B — A market value adjustment can increase or decrease certain surrender/withdrawal values based on changes in market interest rates under contract terms.
38. A — When a life policy is surrendered, gain above the policyowner's adjusted cost basis is generally taxable as ordinary income.
39. A — Premium mode is the frequency of payment, such as annual, semiannual, quarterly, or monthly; more frequent modes can produce a higher total annual outlay.
40. B — Combines term coverage for specified family members under the base insured's policy.
41. B — When required, a statement of good health confirms no material health change before coverage becomes effective or a policy is delivered.
42. B — Convertible term permits conversion to a permanent policy without new evidence of insurability, within contract limits.
43. C — Failed eligibility determination = 5-day notice.
44. A — Renewable term permits renewal for another term without new evidence of insurability, subject to contract limits and higher attained-age premiums.
45. A — A contract must have a lawful purpose.
46. C — Modified life typically uses a lower premium for an initial period followed by a higher level premium thereafter.
47. D — License-required activity → compensation rules matter.
48. A — A licensee reports a legal name or address change within 30 days.
49. D — The producer report supplies underwriting observations not necessarily answered by the applicant, such as relevant personal or financial information observed by the producer.
50. A — You keep the risk.
51. A — Before annuitization, withdrawals from a nonqualified annuity with gain are generally treated as earnings first (LIFO) until gain is exhausted; taxable earnings are ordinary income.
52. A — Proceeds are paid in installments for a specified period; the payment amount is determined by the proceeds, interest, and chosen time period.
53. B — A resident producer generally completes 24 hours of approved continuing education in each 2-year review period.
54. D — The primary beneficiary has first claim to policy proceeds when the insured dies.
55. C — Traditional IRA contributions may be deductible depending on circumstances; earnings generally grow tax-deferred and taxable distributions are generally ordinary income.
56. C — Sales presentations must be truthful and not misleading, and required disclosures must be provided.
57. C — A person may not use boycott, coercion, or intimidation to create or maintain an unreasonable restraint of insurance trade.
58. C — A declared dividend may purchase one-year term insurance, often increasing temporary death protection.
59. D — If the producer is found ineligible for appointment, the director notifies the insurer within 5 days of that determination.
60. D — A policy summary presents key information about the proposed policy in a standardized consumer-oriented form.
61. C — Insurance advertising may not contain statements that are false, deceptive, or misleading about an insurer or policy.
62. B — Interest-sensitive whole life reflects current interest or mortality assumptions within contract limits while retaining whole-life characteristics.
63. B — Decreasing term has a death benefit that declines over time and is often used for decreasing obligations such as debt.
64. A — A contract requires an offer and an acceptance that agree on the essential terms.
65. D — A producer may submit written comments concerning a termination notice within 30 days after receiving it.
66. D — Insurance contracts are contracts of adhesion because the insurer drafts the policy and the applicant generally accepts or rejects it rather than negotiating wording.
67. D — Juvenile life insures a child's life and may include a payor benefit rider to protect premium payments if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined.
68. B — A qualified plan meets Internal Revenue Code requirements and receives tax advantages; distributions are taxed according to the plan and contribution type.
69. D — Straight life whole life uses level premiums payable for life and provides permanent death protection with guaranteed cash value according to the contract.
70. A — An investigative consumer or inspection report may contain information about character, reputation, finances, habits, or lifestyle, subject to consumer-reporting rules.
71. B — Life insurance can provide cash at death when an estate may otherwise hold illiquid assets.
72. C — The needs approach estimates specific survivor lump-sum and income needs, then subtracts available assets and resources.
73. A — An insurer generally reports termination of a producer business relationship within 30 days after the effective date.
74. A — Permanent life insurance can build cash value that may be accessed subject to policy terms.
75. D — Annuities are designed primarily to address the risk of outliving assets by converting value into income that can last for life.
76. D — Consideration is the value exchanged. In life insurance, the applicant's premium and statements are consideration; the insurer's promise is its consideration.
77. A — A conditional receipt may make coverage effective as of a specified date if the applicant satisfies the receipt's conditions.
78. B — On juvenile insurance, the payor benefit rider can waive premiums if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined while the child is still within the rider's covered period.
79. A — An insurable risk is generally definite, measurable, accidental from the insured's standpoint, economically feasible, and part of a large enough pool for prediction.
80. A — Risk retention means keeping responsibility for some or all of a potential loss.
81. B — Preferred applicants present lower-than-average expected mortality and may qualify for more favorable rates.
82. D — An individual life policy generally becomes incontestable after it has been in force during the insured's lifetime for 2 years, subject to statutory exceptions such as nonpayment of premium.
83. D — A deferred annuity has an accumulation period before income payments begin.
84. B — On certain universal life policies, this rider may waive monthly mortality/cost-of-insurance charges during qualifying disability rather than waiving a fixed premium.
85. A — A participating policy may pay policy dividends if declared; dividends are not guaranteed.
86. D — Michigan law generally requires written notice at least 30 days before termination of coverage under the life-policy rule, subject to an exception for certain in-person premium collection.
87. D — A permanent policyowner may borrow against available cash value; unpaid loan balance and interest reduce policy values/death proceeds and can cause lapse if excessive.
88. C — Single-premium whole life is funded by one large premium and is then paid-up, subject to tax rules such as MEC treatment if applicable.
89. C — The Medical Information Bureau provides coded information reported by member insurers that can alert an underwriter to possible undisclosed medical or application history; it does not make the underwriting decision.
90. D — Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
91. C — The owner terminates the permanent policy and receives available cash surrender value, ending the death protection.
92. B — Express authority is specifically granted to a producer by the insurer, often in the agency agreement.
93. C — A qualifying lapsed life policy can generally be reinstated within 3 years under the statute, with satisfactory evidence of insurability and payment of arrears plus interest.
94. C — Life insurance can supply funds to help preserve estate assets rather than forcing their sale to meet expenses or obligations.
95. C — Life insurance is personal in the sense that underwriting and the contract are based on the individual risk and relationship, not merely property ownership.
96. C — Agency contract or first application starts a 15-day filing clock.
97. C — Universal life is flexible-premium permanent insurance with an account value, mortality/expense charges, and an adjustable death benefit subject to contract rules.
98. A — A mutual insurer is owned by policyholders; participating policyholders may be eligible for non-guaranteed policy dividends.
99. A — Risk sharing distributes potential loss among multiple parties.
100. A — When an insurer requires a medical examination or lab test for underwriting, the insurer generally bears the cost.

Mock Form 3 — 100 questions

Target: 90+ without notes. Passing benchmark: 72. Strong readiness gate: 90+ twice, every domain ≥85%.

1. Which statement BEST describes Adjustable life?
  • A. Joint life covers two lives and pays the death benefit at the first insured's death.
  • B. Adjustable life allows changes to items such as premium, face amount, or coverage period within policy limits, blending term/permanent flexibility.
  • C. When qualifying group life coverage terminates, an eligible employee generally has 31 days to apply for an individual policy without new evidence of insurability, subject to statutory terms.
  • D. In group life, the policyholder (often an employer) owns the master contract and covered individuals receive certificates of insurance.
2. Income starts soon. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Flexible-premium deferred annuity
  • B. Deferred annuity
  • C. Immediate annuity
  • D. SPIA
3. Which statement BEST describes Stock insurer?
  • A. Express authority is specifically granted to a producer by the insurer, often in the agency agreement.
  • B. A stock insurer is owned by shareholders, who provide capital and may receive profits as dividends on stock.
  • C. Consideration is the value exchanged. In life insurance, the applicant's premium and statements are consideration; the insurer's promise is its consideration.
  • D. Apparent authority exists when the insurer's actions cause a third party reasonably to believe the producer has authority.
4. Which statement BEST describes Replacement?
  • A. Permanent life insurance can build cash value that may be accessed subject to policy terms.
  • B. Replacement occurs when new life insurance is purchased and an existing policy is or will be lapsed, surrendered, forfeited, assigned, or otherwise materially affected as defined by replacement rules.
  • C. Life insurance can replace income or provide funds for dependents after the insured's death.
  • D. Life insurance can supply funds to help preserve estate assets rather than forcing their sale to meet expenses or obligations.
5. Which statement BEST describes Rebating?
  • A. Insurers may not unfairly discriminate between individuals of the same class and equal life expectancy in rates, benefits, or contract terms.
  • B. Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.
  • C. Offering an inducement or valuable benefit not specified in the contract as an incentive to buy insurance is generally rebating/illegal inducement.
  • D. Knowingly making false statements that damage another insurer's financial condition or reputation is an unfair practice.
6. Which statement BEST describes Renewable term?
  • A. Limited-pay whole life becomes paid-up after premiums are paid for a specified shorter period while coverage continues for life.
  • B. Decreasing term has a death benefit that declines over time and is often used for decreasing obligations such as debt.
  • C. Graded premium policies start with lower premiums that increase on a schedule before leveling at a higher amount.
  • D. Renewable term permits renewal for another term without new evidence of insurability, subject to contract limits and higher attained-age premiums.
7. Which statement BEST describes Risk?
  • A. Risk retention means keeping responsibility for some or all of a potential loss.
  • B. Risk is uncertainty concerning the possibility of loss.
  • C. Risk transfer shifts the financial consequences of loss to another party; insurance is a classic method.
  • D. Speculative risk includes the possibility of loss, no loss, or gain and is generally not insurable in the same way as pure risk.
8. Cash value grows without annual current tax in the usual case. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Policy loans
  • B. Life surrender
  • C. Life policy dividends
  • D. Life cash value growth
9. Which statement BEST describes Fraud?
  • A. Estoppel prevents a party from asserting a right when its prior conduct reasonably caused another party to rely to their detriment.
  • B. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
  • C. Fraud generally requires intentional deception designed to secure an unfair or unlawful gain.
  • D. Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
10. Which statement BEST describes Life insurance advertising?
  • A. A buyer's guide is consumer education intended to help compare and understand life insurance or annuity products.
  • B. A life insurance illustration shows policy values under stated assumptions and must distinguish guaranteed from non-guaranteed elements.
  • C. Life insurance advertising must not create false impressions about benefits, costs, guarantees, or an insurer's identity.
  • D. Applicants and insureds have protections concerning collection, use, and disclosure of insurance information.
11. Which statement BEST describes Policy delivery?
  • A. An investigative consumer or inspection report may contain information about character, reputation, finances, habits, or lifestyle, subject to consumer-reporting rules.
  • B. When an insurer requires a medical examination or lab test for underwriting, the insurer generally bears the cost.
  • C. At delivery, the producer should explain the policy, collect any required premium, and obtain any required statement of continued good health if circumstances require it.
  • D. The producer report supplies underwriting observations not necessarily answered by the applicant, such as relevant personal or financial information observed by the producer.
12. Which statement BEST describes Express authority?
  • A. Express authority is specifically granted to a producer by the insurer, often in the agency agreement.
  • B. A contract must have a lawful purpose.
  • C. Consideration is the value exchanged. In life insurance, the applicant's premium and statements are consideration; the insurer's promise is its consideration.
  • D. Apparent authority exists when the insurer's actions cause a third party reasonably to believe the producer has authority.
13. Which statement BEST describes Joint life / first-to-die?
  • A. Renewable term permits renewal for another term without new evidence of insurability, subject to contract limits and higher attained-age premiums.
  • B. Juvenile life insures a child's life and may include a payor benefit rider to protect premium payments if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined.
  • C. Decreasing term has a death benefit that declines over time and is often used for decreasing obligations such as debt.
  • D. Joint life covers two lives and pays the death benefit at the first insured's death.
14. Which statement BEST describes Survivorship / second-to-die?
  • A. Level term provides a level death benefit for a stated term; premiums may be level for a guaranteed period depending on the contract.
  • B. Survivorship life covers two lives and pays after the second insured dies.
  • C. Convertible term permits conversion to a permanent policy without new evidence of insurability, within contract limits.
  • D. Straight life whole life uses level premiums payable for life and provides permanent death protection with guaranteed cash value according to the contract.

  • C. Applicants and insureds have protections concerning collection, use, and disclosure of insurance information.
  • D. Life insurance can provide cash at death when an estate may otherwise hold illiquid assets.
16. Which statement BEST describes Statement of good health?

  • B. An Attending Physician Statement provides detailed medical information from an applicant's physician when the insurer needs more health detail.
  • C. The Medical Information Bureau provides coded information reported by member insurers that can alert an underwriter to possible undisclosed medical or application history; it does not make the underwriting decision.
  • D. Preferred applicants present lower-than-average expected mortality and may qualify for more favorable rates.
17. Which statement BEST describes Preferred risk?
  • A. Preferred applicants present lower-than-average expected mortality and may qualify for more favorable rates.
  • B. Variable life and variable annuity products involve securities regulation in addition to insurance regulation; securities licensing/registration rules can apply.
  • C. Life insurance advertising must not create false impressions about benefits, costs, guarantees, or an insurer's identity.
  • D. Substandard applicants present higher-than-average expected mortality but may still be insurable with a rating, extra premium, or other terms.
18. Which statement BEST describes Convertible term?

  • C. Single-premium whole life is funded by one large premium and is then paid-up, subject to tax rules such as MEC treatment if applicable.
  • D. Modified life typically uses a lower premium for an initial period followed by a higher level premium thereafter.

  • C. Variable annuity
  • D. Annuity death benefit
82. Which statement BEST describes Mutual insurer?
  • A. Contracting parties must have legal capacity to contract.
  • B. A mutual insurer is owned by policyholders; participating policyholders may be eligible for non-guaranteed policy dividends.
  • C. A contract requires an offer and an acceptance that agree on the essential terms.
  • D. Implied authority is not expressly stated but is reasonably necessary to carry out express authority.
83. Death benefit generally income-tax-free. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Nonqualified annuity withdrawal — LIFO
  • B. Death benefit
  • C. Nonqualified annuity accumulation
  • D. Interest on death proceeds
84. Which statement BEST describes Pure risk?
  • A. Risk avoidance eliminates the activity or exposure that creates the risk.
  • B. Pure risk involves only the possibility of loss or no loss, not a chance of gain.
  • C. Risk sharing distributes potential loss among multiple parties.
  • D. An insurable risk is generally definite, measurable, accidental from the insured's standpoint, economically feasible, and part of a large enough pool for prediction.
85. Which statement BEST describes Solicitation and sales presentations?
  • A. Replacement occurs when new life insurance is purchased and an existing policy is or will be lapsed, surrendered, forfeited, assigned, or otherwise materially affected as defined by replacement rules.
  • B. Sales presentations must be truthful and not misleading, and required disclosures must be provided.
  • C. The guaranty association is a safety-net mechanism for covered obligations of insolvent member insurers, subject to statutory limits; it is not a sales guarantee or reason to recommend a policy.
  • D. A policy summary presents key information about the proposed policy in a standardized consumer-oriented form.
86. Lifetime only; stops at death. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Life with period certain
  • B. Straight life annuity
  • C. Longevity risk
  • D. Joint and survivor annuity
87. Exclusion must come from policy language/law. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Interest on proceeds
  • B. Exclusions and restrictions
  • C. Primary beneficiary
  • D. Contingent beneficiary
88. Life policy rider pays disability income. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Accelerated death benefit
  • B. Payor benefit
  • C. Disability income rider
  • D. Long-term-care rider
89. Money moves trustee to trustee; owner never takes possession. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Life cash value growth
  • B. Qualified retirement plan
  • C. 1035 exchange
  • D. Trustee-to-trustee transfer
90. Pays until the second of two lives ends. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Joint-and-survivor income
  • B. Reduced paid-up
  • C. Extended term
  • D. Cash surrender
91. Which statement BEST describes Conditional contract?
  • A. Waiver is the voluntary relinquishment of a known right.
  • B. An application statement made to the best of the applicant's knowledge is generally treated as a representation rather than an absolute warranty.
  • C. Concealment is the intentional failure to disclose a material fact that should be disclosed.
  • D. The insurer's obligation to perform depends on specified policy conditions being satisfied.
92. Which statement BEST describes Law of large numbers?
  • A. As the number of similar exposure units increases, actual losses tend to become more predictable relative to expected losses.
  • B. A mutual insurer is owned by policyholders; participating policyholders may be eligible for non-guaranteed policy dividends.
  • C. A contract requires an offer and an acceptance that agree on the essential terms.
  • D. Implied authority is not expressly stated but is reasonably necessary to carry out express authority.
93. Which statement BEST describes Unfair discrimination?
  • A. Knowingly presenting materially false information in support of an insurance application or claim may constitute insurance fraud.
  • B. The director may order a person to stop conduct that violates insurance law or an applicable order/rule.
  • C. Insurers may not unfairly discriminate between individuals of the same class and equal life expectancy in rates, benefits, or contract terms.
  • D. Commissions for insurance sales are generally paid only as permitted by licensing law; unlicensed persons may not be paid for activities requiring a producer license.
94. Which statement BEST describes Nonparticipating policy?
  • A. Premium mode is the frequency of payment, such as annual, semiannual, quarterly, or monthly; more frequent modes can produce a higher total annual outlay.
  • B. A nonparticipating policy does not pay policy dividends.
  • C. At delivery, the producer should explain the policy, collect any required premium, and obtain any required statement of continued good health if circumstances require it.
  • D. The application is a primary source of underwriting information and becomes part of the contract when attached as required.
95. SMALLER face amount, PERMANENT, no more premiums. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Withdrawal / partial surrender
  • B. Policy loan
  • C. Automatic premium loan
  • D. Reduced paid-up
96. Which statement BEST describes Forgery?
  • A. Grounds can include material misstatements, fraud, misappropriation, dishonest practices, or violating insurance laws and orders.
  • B. An insurer generally files a producer appointment notice within 15 days after the agency contract is executed or the first application is submitted.
  • C. Nonpublic personal and insurance information is subject to privacy, notice, and disclosure restrictions.
  • D. Forging another person's name on an insurance-related document is grounds for producer discipline.
97. Which statement BEST describes Juvenile life?
  • A. Limited-pay whole life becomes paid-up after premiums are paid for a specified shorter period while coverage continues for life.
  • B. Renewable term permits renewal for another term without new evidence of insurability, subject to contract limits and higher attained-age premiums.
  • C. Juvenile life insures a child's life and may include a payor benefit rider to protect premium payments if the payor dies or becomes disabled as defined.
  • D. Decreasing term has a death benefit that declines over time and is often used for decreasing obligations such as debt.
98. Which statement BEST describes Needs approach?
  • A. Term provides temporary death protection without cash value; permanent coverage is designed to last for life and commonly builds cash value.
  • B. A nonparticipating policy does not pay policy dividends.
  • C. A business may own and benefit from life insurance on a key employee whose death would cause financial loss.
  • D. The needs approach estimates specific survivor lump-sum and income needs, then subtracts available assets and resources.
99. Which statement BEST describes Continuing education — 24 hours / 2 years?
  • A. A producer reports a covered administrative action within 30 days after final disposition.
  • B. Using misleading comparisons or misrepresentations to induce a policyowner to lapse, surrender, forfeit, or replace existing coverage is twisting.
  • C. Premiums and other money received in a producer's professional role must be handled in trust and not converted to personal use.
  • D. A resident producer generally completes 24 hours of approved continuing education in each 2-year review period.
100. Backup beneficiary. Which term or rule BEST matches?
  • A. Irrevocable beneficiary
  • B. Class designation
  • C. Revocable beneficiary
  • D. Contingent beneficiary

Why each answer is right

1. B — Policy can be adjusted as needs change.
2. C — An immediate annuity begins income payments soon after purchase, generally within one payment interval and no later than about one year depending on contract convention.
3. B — Stock = shareholders.
4. B — New policy causes old policy to be dropped/changed.
5. C — Off-the-contract incentive = rebate.
6. D — Renew without new medical proof.
7. B — Uncertainty of loss = risk.
8. D — Cash value growth inside a life insurance policy is generally tax-deferred while it remains in the policy.
9. C — Intentional deceit for gain.
10. C — Ads cannot exaggerate guarantees.
11. C — Delivery = explain, collect, confirm conditions.
12. A — Explicitly granted = express.
13. D — Two insureds; first death pays.
14. B — Two insureds; second death pays.
15. B — Consumer education before/around purchase.
16. A — Health changed before delivery? This can matter.
17. A — Better-than-average mortality risk.
18. A — Term → permanent without proving insurability again.
19. C — False attack on competitor = defamation-type unfair practice.
20. D — Pays an additional amount if death meets the rider's definition of qualifying accidental death and exclusions do not apply.
21. A — Misleading replacement = twisting.
22. B — Move or legal-name change → 30.
23. A — Provides a return of specified premiums under the rider's terms, often if the insured survives a stated term; it is not the same as cash value from whole life.
24. C — A specified dollar amount is paid periodically until proceeds and interest are exhausted.
25. C — Pay level premiums for life.
26. A — Group ends → 31-day conversion.
27. A — Multiple parties share the burden.
28. D — One enforceable promise: insurer.
29. C — If age or gender is misstated, benefits are generally adjusted to the amount the paid premium would have purchased at the correct age or gender, rather than automatically voiding the policy.
30. C — Buy insurance → transfer.
31. A — Insurer-requested exam → insurer pays.
32. D — A declared dividend may be taken in cash.
33. D — Term coverage with level face amount.
34. C — A deferred annuity may accept multiple premiums over time rather than a single premium.
35. D — Policy changes generally must meet the contract's modification requirements and be made by an authorized officer rather than informally by a producer.
36. D — Producer gathers; insurer decides.
37. D — Pressure that restrains insurance trade = prohibited.
38. B — DIFS eligibility check: think 30.
39. C — Something of value from each contracting side.
40. D — Creates money at death.
41. D — Fiduciary = funds held for someone else.
42. D — A buyer's guide is consumer education intended to help compare and understand life insurance or annuity products.
43. C — Insurer writes it; insured adheres.
44. A — Joint life covers two lives and pays the death benefit at the first insured's death.
45. B — The insurer pays the proceeds in a lump sum.
46. B — One rider can cover eligible children.
47. D — Before annuitization, many annuities provide a contractual death benefit to the beneficiary based on account value or a stated formula.
48. B — Policy + attached application = contract.
49. D — Ad must tell the truth.
50. A — Administrative final disposition → 30.
51. D — Index-linked crediting, not direct stock ownership.
52. B — Third-party background/lifestyle report.
53. A — Exclusion must come from policy language/law.
54. A — Higher-risk people are more eager to insure.
55. B — A fixed annuity is backed by the insurer's general account; the insurer bears investment risk and guarantees interest/values as stated in the contract.
56. C — If an insurer holds proceeds and pays interest, the interest is separate from the death benefit and may have different tax treatment.
57. B — Know the right, choose to give it up.
58. C — Business protects itself from loss of a key worker.
59. B — False policy claim used to sell = misrepresentation.
60. C — Cease and desist = stop the prohibited conduct.
61. B — Allows the insured to purchase specified additional coverage at certain times without new evidence of insurability, subject to rider limits.
62. A — Earnings in a nonqualified annuity generally grow tax-deferred during accumulation.
63. D — Protect people who rely on the insured.
64. C — Replacement occurs when new life insurance is purchased and an existing policy is or will be lapsed, surrendered, forfeited, assigned, or otherwise materially affected as defined by replacement rules.
65. B — Protect nonpublic consumer information.
66. B — Master policy vs individual contract.
67. D — A beneficiary can be designated by a class, such as 'children,' with proceeds distributed according to policy/law at the insured's death.
68. A — Variable = insurance + securities oversight.
69. C — Bad application info, theft/fraud, or insurance-law violations can jeopardize the license.
70. A — 24 total; 3 ethics.
71. D — Absolute assignment transfers all ownership rights in the policy to another party.
72. C — Guaranteed vs non-guaranteed must stay clear.
73. B — Employer/organization holds master policy.
74. B — Provides term insurance on eligible children under the base policy, commonly convertible under rider terms.
75. A — Definite + measurable + accidental + large pool.
76. C — Policy-at-a-glance document.
77. D — Don't do the risky activity.
78. B — Taxable IRA distributions before age 59½ are generally subject to a 10% additional federal tax unless an exception applies.
79. B — Contract centers on the person/risk.
80. C — If the estate is beneficiary, proceeds become part of the estate and may be exposed to estate administration and creditor issues.
81. A — A surrender charge is a contract charge that may apply to withdrawals or surrender during an early contract period.
82. B — Mutual = policyholders.
83. B — Life insurance death proceeds paid by reason of the insured's death are generally excluded from the beneficiary's gross income, subject to exceptions such as transfer-for-value rules.
84. B — Loss or no loss; no upside.
85. B — Sell accurately; disclose properly.
86. B — Payments continue for the annuitant's lifetime and generally stop at death, producing the highest life-only income among comparable options because no refund/guarantee period is built in.
87. B — Life policies may contain lawful exclusions or restrictions such as certain aviation, war/military, or rider-specific limitations, but exclusions must be stated in the contract.
88. C — A disability income rider can provide a specified income benefit if the insured meets the rider's disability definition.
89. D — A direct transfer from one IRA trustee to another is not treated as a rollover distribution to the owner and is generally tax-free.
90. A — Income continues while either of two annuitants/beneficiaries remains alive under the selected option.
91. D — Coverage/payment depends on conditions.
92. A — Bigger similar pool = better prediction.
93. C — Same class + equal life expectancy should receive fair treatment.
94. B — No policy dividends.
95. D — Cash value purchases a smaller amount of fully paid permanent insurance with no further premiums required.
96. D — Fake signature = serious licensing violation.
97. C — Child insured; payor rider may protect premiums.
98. D — List needs, subtract resources.
99. D — CE = 24 in 2.
100. D — The contingent beneficiary receives proceeds if the primary beneficiary cannot or does not receive them under the policy terms.
Agency testing gate

12. The 90% readiness protocol

A 90% practice score is useful only if the questions are unseen and the candidate can explain the rule. This protocol is designed to stop inflated “I memorized the bank” scores.

Gate 1 — Coverage
Candidate can explain every item in the official-outline audit without notes.
Gate 2 — Confusion pairs
Candidate can distinguish every trap pair aloud: express/implied/apparent, extended/reduced paid-up, fixed amount/fixed period, fixed/variable, etc.
Gate 3 — Fresh Form #1
100 questions, 120 minutes max, closed book, no hints. Target ≥90.
Gate 4 — Domain floor
No scored domain below 85%. A high overall score must not hide a weak 21-question section.
Gate 5 — Miss review
For every miss or guess, candidate states the tested rule and why each competing answer is wrong.
Gate 6 — Fresh Form #2
Different unseen 100-question form, closed book. Target ≥90 again.
Important: Meeting this gate cannot legally or honestly guarantee a live PSI score. It is a deliberately stricter readiness standard designed to create margin above the 72% cut score and reduce form-to-form volatility.

13. Source notes — verification only

Final verification stack — September 25, 2026:
  • PSI Michigan Life Producer Series 16-65 public outline: 100 scored items, 2 hours, 72 correct to pass; domains 17/18/21/12/21/6/5.
  • Michigan DIFS Examination Information / Candidate Bulletin: PSI is Michigan's exam vendor; prelicensing completion and passing exam results are each valid 12 months.
  • Michigan DIFS resident-producer licensing: approved Life prelicensing may be completed through a 20-hour classroom course or approved self-study; resident application validity is 180 days.
  • Michigan Insurance Code / DIFS appointment guidance: appointment filing 15 days; eligibility check up to 30 days; ineligibility notice 5 days; termination-for-cause reporting/copy/comment timelines 30/15/30.
  • DIFS CE guidance: 24 credits every 2 years with at least 3 ethics; current carryover guidance allows up to 12 excess credits after the minimum is satisfied.
  • DIFS annuity best-interest guidance: one-time 4-hour annuity training for producers licensed on/after June 29, 2021 before annuity sales; consumer-best-interest and profile-information duties apply to recommendations.
  • DIFS LTC training guidance: one-time 8-hour LTC training plus at least 4 hours per 2-year compliance period for producers selling LTC.
  • IRS 2026 guidance: 2026 IRA contribution limit $7,500; age-50+ catch-up $1,100; nonqualified annuity pre-annuitization withdrawals generally allocate earnings first; current RMD ages follow SECURE 2.0 birth-year rules.

You do not need to open these to study. The tested rules are already summarized above.

Michigan DIFS — Examination Information

Michigan DIFS is the state licensing authority and links candidates to the current PSI bulletin, content outlines, cut scores, and exam performance reports.

PSI — Michigan Life Producer Series 16-65 Content Outline

Current public blueprint used here: 100 scored questions across 7 domains; the score reports provided match the same 17/18/21/12/21/6/5 distribution.

PSI — Michigan Candidate Information Bulletin

Candidate rules, scheduling, identification, test-center procedures, and PSI notices. PSI may include experimental questions that are not scored.

Michigan Insurance Code — Chapter 500

Primary Michigan statutory source for producer licensing, appointments, discipline, life-policy provisions, and other Michigan-specific rules.

Michigan DIFS — Resident Producer Licensing

Michigan prelicensing and licensing process. DIFS states the prelicensing completion certificate is valid 12 months and the corresponding exam must be passed in that period.

IRS — Life Insurance / Annuity / IRA guidance

Federal tax treatment in the tax section is summarized from IRS guidance: death proceeds are generally excluded from income; interest is taxable; annuity gains are tax-deferred until distribution; IRA and rollover rules have separate requirements.

Michigan Life and Health Examinations Report for 2025

Official report showed 467 items in the MI Life Producer item bank during 2025, with an average item percent-correct of 62.69%. This is strong evidence that candidates can receive materially different forms drawn from a large pool; it does not mean all 467 remain unchanged in 2026.
